WRWALKER's `crossJoin` is its static twin, §1.10) · `template_alternation` (two settlements, one season, the same sentence — `phrase-repetition.mjs`'s cell). **The validator** `validateReaderReport(report, manifest, resolveLocator)` (§1.7 codes) READS the corpus directory (resolving a locator is a file read) — it is a script, not a unit test; the unit test proves it on in-memory synthetic manifests and plants. **The control campaign — the no-fork pin:** `rr-control-w0` composes the soak's OWN fixture (four archetypes, seed `w0-soak`, `full_simulation`, no golden row) through `composeReaderRegion` and Car 1's proof asserts its `yearlyWorldHashes[0]` EQUALS `yearlyHashes[0]` of `node scripts/audit/whole-world-soak.mjs --years 1 --json` at the same tip (⟦G0-64⟧ **SETTLED BY EXECUTION AND THE REMEDY IS UNNECESSARY.** The soak hashes `sha256(JSON.stringify({ worldState, regionalGraph, settlements }))` — three keys in that literal order, `settlements` being the THREADED saves' settlements post-`settlementUpdates` merge, never the fixture's originals (`whole-world-soak.mjs:453-457`, `sha` at `:187`); five candidate objects were hashed at year 1 and **only the three-key composite matches** (`2409ea4ec9a9f788ecca243d2ea480909463b0ec859a1bf4a5c9a08347dc8daf`), with `hashedObjectKeys` returning `["worldState","regionalGraph","settlements"]`. `sha(worldState)` ALONE is `da2d4a3c…` and is NOT it. ⭐ **And the equality proof is PRE-PAID: the REAL soak run in the dock at `--years 1` reproduces the control campaign's hash EXACTLY** (`yearlyHashes[0]` `2409ea4e…` both sides; `directFirstResultSha256` `848e7272…` both sides, TRUE_EXIT 0), so Car 1's proof is DISCHARGEABLE and the exact composite it must hash is pinned — hashing `result`, or `worldState` alone, silently produces a different, plausible-looking hash. All 30 yearly hashes were distinct: the world genuinely moves every year (READERREVIEW Car 0 receipt, `$SP/laneG0RR-receipt.md`) §4. The volume's contingency, retired: PLAUSIBLE that the soak hashes a different object by a field — Car 0 P0.5 names the hashed object; if it differs, a second control hash of the soak's object is added; the claim that survives is "the runner's yearly loop reproduces the soak's world byte for byte" — the fork test the determinism law demands). Cost: one soak year ≈ 27–38 s.

**Cost and corpus size (measured figures, PLAUSIBLE arithmetic).** ⟦G0-63⟧ **RE-PRICED DOWNWARD BY MORE THAN AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE, MEASURED: one full 30-year 4-settlement campaign costs 61.88 s real (1.02 min; 68.84 user, 2.03 sys), not 14–19 min** — of which the advance loop is 60,997 ms and the fixture build (4 × `generateSettlementPipeline`) 157 ms; peak heap 450,141,384 B (≈ 450 MB), peak RSS 786,923,520 B (≈ 787 MB, `/usr/bin/time -l` agreeing exactly). ⚠ **Per-year cost FALLS** — 2,673 ms (y1) → 1,851 ms (y30), mean 2,033 ms — consistent with the two retention caps bounding the working set; no runaway over 30 years. ⚠ **SCOPE, stated plainly:** this is the ADVANCE + composite hashing only; it excludes document rendering (P0.1 measures that separately at **3.46 s per settlement for all four PDF variants**, `canon_dossier` alone 1.58 s) and the soak's own per-year observation legs, some of which the reader runner will pay. ⇒ **the 11-campaign roster costs ≈ 11 min of advance SERIAL, before rendering and with no parallelism at all**; Car 2's "> 45 min for one campaign" STOP is ~44× away from firing, and §11.4's bill and §5.7 R6's parallelism risk are both re-priced here (READERREVIEW Car 0 receipt, `$SP/laneG0RR-receipt.md`) §3. The volume's believed figures, for the record: one year of a 4-settlement `full_simulation` world ≈ 27–38 s on this Mac (SOAKCHAIN's on-disk receipts); 30 years ≈ 14–19 min per campaign; rendering is negligible beside it (525 generations ≈ 9 s; a dossier render ≈ seconds). The default roster of 11 campaigns ≈ 2.5–3.5 h serial, embarrassingly parallel by process (`--only`), ≈ 45 min on four processes. The reading, not the rendering, is the dominant cost: 11 campaigns × 7 reader seats + refuters.

> **JUDGMENT (READERREVIEW, amended ⟦A51 L1⟧): SIX golden rows (one per tier — the GOLDEN charter's Arm-3 idiom) rendered under the DEFAULT preset `realistic_regional` + TWO fresh regions under the world-alive presets (`full_simulation`, `dramatic_campaign`; `living_realm` is unread at 11 and is RR-4's first widening) + TWO preview twins + the control = 11, over "the golden corpus seeds + a few fresh" read as all 525 — because 525 × 30 years ≈ 130–170 h of rendering and 525 × 7 seats is not a panel, and tiers are the axis along which every rubric system's density changes (a thorp has no ladder; a metropolis has three factions) — vetoable; the roster is a frozen literal the owner widens by adding keys (each must exist in the golden manifest — a pin).**

### §5.3 Laws honoured
THE PROMISE → no `src/` byte in Cars 0–2; the review phase sits AFTER the freeze, so every fix car carries `outputMoving` and `goldensMoved[]` as MEASURED fields (the golden family + the REQUIRED `--verify` on the WORLD documents — ⟦A10 P5⟧ `outputMoving = goldensMoved.length > 0 || worldDocsMoved`) and `validateBacklogRow` refuses `outputMoving === true` — not merely `goldensMoved.length > 0` — without a `shiftRecord` (`action: 're-record'`, owner — §1.9) or an `ownerRow`; a `PROSE_RENDER`/DISPLAY fix that moves only rendered-document hashes and no world hash is a surface change, recorded as such; a fix that moves a WORLD hash is a same-seed shift whatever its car class says, and the class is decided by the WRITER FILE (⟦A60 L9⟧). DARK BY CONSTRUCTION → no mechanism, no flag minted; the LIT-PREVIEW corpus is an overlay on a scratch object through `composeSoakRules`; determinism is the manifest's bits + `--verify` + the control campaign's equality with the soak's year-1 hash. FINITE SEMANTICS → closed enums, a frozen contradiction roster, snake_case refusal codes; the reader's `prose` is the only free text and the validator never consumes it; no AI clerk is in the loop. THE NEWS ADDRESS LAW → the citation shape IS the four parts; Q-ADR-1 scores every citation; the manifest carries `measureAddressChain` per campaign-year; `resolveSubject` is the estate's ONE resolver ("Nothing is composed from prose: the join is by TYPED ID", `realmEntityWeb.js:16-21`). THE ENGINE CEILING → 0 B; every Car 4 row carries `chunk` and `firstPaintDelta` measured. THE INSTRUMENT ESTATE → no check-chain step, no npm script (the 30-year render belongs in NO gate — build-efficiency §7); two test files pay the three-census bill; **the citation law bound the other way round**: a reader's citation string IS the artifact and is checked by RESOLUTION against the corpus, never by grep of source. THE TWO PDF RENDERERS → `name_drift`/`cross_document_disagreement` RECORD the disagreement across `dossier-*.vm` and `campaign-pdf`/`worldbook.*`; the carClass is `LIGHTING_DESK` (never lane-mintable); the row cites the owner's design row (`TUNING-DESK.md:560-570`) and pre-empts nothing; the protocol's own JSON/text outputs carry every codepoint verbatim. SEQUENCING → Cars 0–2 are instruments; Car 3 IS the owner-directed review phase (§879.6); Car 4's output-moving rows ride the door. OWNER-GATED → §5.6. THE LEGIBILITY LAW and GAME-GRADE UX (owner 2026-07-22) → the `formula_leak` value, ⟦A54 L4⟧ the REGISTER axis (the voice bible's hard rules made a scored, partly machine-checked axis) and the REGULAR-HUMAN stance of the reader brief (the legibility-audit workflow's "Judge each surface AS THAT REGULAR HUMAN"). STATE, NEVER FATE → `ousted_actor_acting` reads OUSTED/corrupt marks, never death; the rubric never asks a reader to find a death.

### §5.6 The landing slot (→ §10)
Cars 0–2 (the instrument) move nothing and must EXIST before the freeze so the corpus can be rendered at the frozen tip. > **JUDGMENT (READERREVIEW): a rider on the W-ARMS gate (which already pays a lighting refreeze for its one new test file — §880.5) over an own gate — because the full gate is 16–25 min and two lighting refreezes cost more than one composed tuple; the risk is the composition class ("a register on one parent governing a file on the other is invisible to both" — the TAIL-F lesson), cured by measuring the tuple at the composed tip with the probe — vetoable; if W-ARMS is vetoed, Cars 0–2 take their own gate after T13.**

**The JUDGMENT's premise is CORRECTED (§13 C3): W-ARMS lands AFTER the freeze, so Cars 0–2 board the HORIZON-DARK consist (after T13, before GOLDEN — §10), paying its ONE lighting refreeze; the design's own fallback ("their own gate after T13") is exactly that slot.** The RENDER (Car 2's run, not its code) happens at the START of the review phase, on the tree after the walk + ONE regen — the world the customer gets (a corpus rendered earlier is re-rendered anyway; the regen is output-moving by definition). ⟦A44 O13⟧ ONE EARLIER render — the LIT-PREVIEW posture only, at HORIZON-DARK's tip, before the wave — reads the dark systems' surfaces so the wave's R9(d) owner rows (fund or defer) and the inventory's lighting cars have reader evidence in hand; it is a scratch overlay world and needs neither the walk nor the regen; the LAUNCH corpus renders after the regen as designed. Car 3 IS the review phase (§879.6: "full systematic comprehension, exhaustive review analysis and fix right before the soak"; seats Fable read / Opus fix per §447/§724). Car 4 lands inside the review phase; output-moving rows through the GOLDEN door (owner) or to the desk. ⟦G0-73⟧ ⛔ **AN OWNER ROW THIS FOLD OPENS AND DOES NOT DECIDE — RR-10, THE LIT-PREVIEW / OPERATIONS GAP.** Car 0 proved that with the FULL espionage conjunction lit and `espionageActive === true`, the layer writes ZERO records over thirty years in a four-settlement realm (⟦G0-61⟧). So the preview corpus cannot make OPERATIONS readable: Q-OPS-1/Q-OPS-2 score `dark_by_flag` on the PREVIEW posture as well as on LAUNCH, which defeats the purpose §5.1 gives the preview corpus ("so 'correct but invisible' and 'dark by flag' can never be confused"). Three options, none a lane's to pick: **(a)** accept it and let the rubric record OPERATIONS as unreadable-at-launch with this receipt as its evidence; **(b)** widen the preview realm or horizon until an accepted peace occurs (unbounded — 30 years already failed at four settlements); **(c)** the preview corpus SEEDS a covert mission directly, which is a fixture, not a lighting overlay. **At minimum `PREVIEW_OVERLAY` must gain `errandSpineEnabled` or its espionage member is dead on arrival** — that much is a chair act, not an owner one, and is folded (⟦G0-61⟧). The row touches RR-8's charter and the §12 owner rows; it is carried to §19.

**Owner-gated, exactly:** opening the phase and accepting its charter (RR-8); every `ENGINE_OWNER` row (RR-9); every output-moving fix after the freeze (a signed `re-record`; regression goldens `enroll` chair-signed vetoably); a war-memory RENDERER (RR-2 — no surface reads `concludedWars` at the tip; the WALK-PROTOCOL §2.11 premise "Concluded wars persist and render" is contradicted at the tip); DM-truth on the dossier NPC card (RR-3 — the leaf's own comment says the DM block is "left for a worldState-bearing surface", `npcComponents.jsx:254-256`: an AUDIENCE policy, not a wiring slip); ⟦CHAIR-R9⟧ FUNDING a residue key the wave could not light (its R9(d) owner row — the LIGHTING-INVENTORY's, evidenced by the preview corpus); the non-Latin design row (CS-1). Not owner-gated (chair's, vetoable): the roster size and members (RR-4 asks anyway), the rubric wording, the two-corpus law, the car-class assignments, the refuter default.

## §6 COVERAGE — the BAND CENSUS: every band of every banded field ≥ N in the golden corpus, widening as a GOLDEN enroll

### §6.0 Thesis
The golden manifest can say THAT a seed's world moved, never WHAT moved in the reader's vocabulary, and nothing in the estate can say whether the 525 frozen worlds ever exercise a band at all — today they do not — ⟦G0-32⟧ **but only ONE of the two, MEASURED at C′: `Wealthy` is 0 over the whole 525; `Struggling` is NOT** (volume "`Struggling` 0" → MEASURED **36 rows**, thorp 12 · hamlet 12 · village 12 — the receipt's zero was true only of the town-and-above slice, so `Struggling` sits 12× over the floor and is struck as a widening target, §4 C11 (COVERAGE Car 0 receipt, `$SP/laneG0COV-receipt.md`)) (CONFIRMED-by-lane: `generatorGoldenMaster.test.js:149-158` "Comfortable 55 · Moderate 12 · Poor 24 · Prosperous 179"; ⟦G0-32⟧ the same control RE-MEASURED at C′ over the 273 town-and-above rows reads **55 · 12 · 24 · 182** and reconciles to the unit — the 3 extra rows carry no criminal institution and return before `corruptionPass`, so nothing moved in prosperity between `6770f878f` and C′ despite the 305-row TE-AGNOSTIC re-record); `heartland` 0 because 523 of 525 rows carry the out-of-vocabulary tokens `civilized`/`safe` straight through `normalizeMonsterThreat` (`monsterThreat.js:55-60` passes any unknown token verbatim; `resolveConfig.js:149-156, :198` writes it); `mountain_pass` 2, `crossroads` 1 → ⟦G0-33⟧ **MEASURED `crossroads` 3 — AT the floor** (`random_trade` is a sentinel resolved BEFORE persistence, so the key histogram's 1 and the persisted `config.tradeRouteAccess`'s 3 legitimately differ; the same shape on terrain — keys give `plains` 84, the census measures 85), `none` 1; ⟦G0-33⟧ **so the first widening set is TWO route bands (`mountain_pass` 2 · `none` 1), not three**. `activeConditions[].severityBand` 0 by construction because `corpus()` sets no stress (base config `:695` carries only the five key fields) → ⟦G0-33⟧ **REFUTED BY EXECUTION: 516 of 525 rows carry an activeCondition** (`medium` 250 · `high` 230 · `critical` 36 · `low` 0) — `corpus()` sets no stress but `resolveConfig` ROLLS one (`config.stressTypes` = `infiltrated` on 480 rows, `famine` on 36, empty on 9), so the field is one of the roster's best-covered rows and only `low` needs a cure (COVERAGE Car 0 receipt, `$SP/laneG0COV-receipt.md`). The BAND CENSUS is an executed instrument over the same 525 rows the golden already regenerates each gate: it derives its denominator from the generator's own closed vocabularies (finite semantics — a band roster is derivable, so the instrument can be TOTAL over it), counts settlements-carrying-each-band, REFUSES any band under a floor N unless a typed exemption names why (and inverts the exemption into a claim: an exempt band that reaches the floor reds until struck), freezes the counts BOUND TO THE MANIFEST'S BYTE-SHA so it is exactly as fresh as the golden and can be refrozen only downstream of a door act, and PROPOSES widenings as door-shaped `enroll` rows found by a budgeted, deterministic, typed seed search. Nothing here moves output; the widening does, through GOLDEN's enroll arm, owner-signed, at the freeze. The second, quieter value: at every legitimate golden shift its refreeze prints the per-band before/after — the T8 record's hand-tallied "12 reached / 12 moved" becomes machinery. The name is **BAND CENSUS** (`bandCensus`); "corpus-coverage" is a RETIRED OSR schema-8 name (`observed-shape-baseline.mjs:218`) and is not used (collision scan over src/tests/scripts/docs: zero hits).

### §6.1 The mechanism
**The denominator — a typed field roster, derived not transcribed.** `BandField` (§1.8): `id` in the §1.3 spelling; `tier: 'persisted'|'derived'`; `read(s) → string[]` DEDUPED per settlement (40 NPCs of importance `minor` = ONE row, 40 occurrences); `vocabulary: { kind: 'exported' (IMPORTED from src, compared exact-set-both-ways against the register's frozen list) | 'pinned' (no frozen src export exists; the list is a CLAIM checked by execution — every observed token must be in it or `unknown-band` reds), home, values }` (`home` is documentation for the reader, never a USE claim — the citation law); `floored` (false ⇒ `whyNotFloored` required); `gatedBy[]` (a field with `gatedBy` never enters the seed search — its proposals are `key-extension` or exempt); `habitat[]` (ordered search hints). THE ROSTER `BAND_FIELDS` (every `home` CONFIRMED-by-lane at `60255ca8e` unless marked): (1) `prosperity on economicState` — `prosperityRank.js#PROSPERITY_LABELS` exported 6; `Wealthy` is `gatedBy ['priorityEconomy>=70-effective']` (`computeBaseProsperity` caps the base at 4, `:241-259`; the only +1 is `econOut >= 70`; `corpus()` never sets `priorityEconomy` ⇒ dial-gated — PLAUSIBLE on the formula; Car 0 (e) settles it) · (2) `label on foodSecurity` — pinned 6 (`foodGenerator.js:340-357`, composed inline); **The floor and its typed reasons.** `BAND_FLOOR_ROWS = 3`, a DRAFT in the register's authored block (`floor: { rows: 3, signedBy: null, ownerRow: null, rationale }`) until the owner signs (the kindPoolWalker precedent: "the owner signs them at the soak redo; the SHAPE is this module's"). ONE constant for every floored field; a per-field floor is refused as a second table. `CENSUS_REASONS` (§1.8; the sorted-array-of-typed-reasons idiom): `under-floor` (a floored cell with `rows < floor` and no exemption → a widening (enroll, owner) or an exemption (chair, cited)) · `exempt-but-reached` (an exemption names a cell whose live `rows >= floor` → strike it in the same commit — "BANK THE WIN"; "A declaration is a CLAIM, not a licence") · `exemption-orphan` · `out-of-vocabulary` (a token outside the vocabulary AND not frozen in `knownDefects`, or its frozen count GREW → a producer cure or a corpus re-key (owner)) · `unknown-band` (a `pinned` vocabulary saw a token it does not list → a finding, never a silent re-pin) · `vocabulary-drift` (an `exported` vocabulary ≠ the frozen list, both ways → re-measure through the ritual) · `count-drift` (any live `rows` ≠ frozen `rows` → a golden shift happened or the tree is dirty; re-measure AFTER the door act; the printed old→new IS the census) · `manifest-unbound` (`sha256(manifest bytes) ≠ corpora[0].manifestSha256`) · `widening-unclosed` (a widening row's key not in the manifest, or a manifest key `corpus()` does not produce).

**The exemption register — authored, cited, inverted into a claim.** `Exemption` (§1.8): `reason` is a TOTAL positive predicate (the M8 `mechanism` pattern); `citation` a `§NNN` row or `path#symbol` (existence-checked for the second form); `mechanism` REQUIRED for `dial-gated` and spelled somewhere under `src/` as a CODE token read through `codeOnly` with STRINGS KEPT — a flag name IS a string, so this is a MINT check (the citation law's second face); `note ≥ 40` (the manifest's own rationale floor); `openedAt`. NO shrink-only ceiling on the exemption COUNT.

**The widening — a data-driven enroll and a budgeted, typed seed search.** (a) `tests/fixtures/generator-corpus-widening.json` (§1.8 `WideningRow[]`, starting EMPTY); `corpus()` appends `readWideningRows()` — a THREE-line edit (`import`, one `rows.push(...)`), title-neutral, HASH-NEUTRAL while empty (arm 2 is the no-op pin at zero cost). Why data, not code: the `aa33eba5` precedent added rows as code with a comment; a DATA row carries what a code row cannot — `cures`, `reason`, `ownerRow`, `proposedBy`, `proposedAtSha` — so the corpus itself obeys THE NEWS ADDRESS LAW. The name avoids `golden` so GOLDEN's `/golden/i` fixture-claim arm (`goldenFreeze.walker.test.js:460-468`) does not convict an unclaimed file; the band-census walker closes it (`widening-unclosed`; `ownerRow` non-blank per row).

(b) `scripts/band-census-propose.mjs` (no npm script; NOT one of OSR's eleven scanner-tool files): loads the register, takes every `under-floor` cell with no exemption, runs `proposeWidening(cell, { generate, budget })`. **Classification first, search second**: a `gatedBy` field never enters the search — its proposal is `key-extension` (a config dimension the 6-segment key cannot carry: `priorityEconomy` for Wealthy, `stressTypes` for severityBand/Famine) or `config-cure` (a token the corpus mis-spells: `heartland`). **Candidates in a FIXED order**: the field's `habitat` fragments (else the 504 grid configs in corpus order) × seeds `bc-<fieldSlug>-<bandSlug>-<i>` (prefix-indexed like the roads hunts `em-${i}`/`cs-${i}`). **Predicate = the band appears in `field.read(generate(row))`** — the REAL pipeline, not a first-draw probe (a band is a whole-pipeline outcome), so the budget is TWO ORDERS below the roads hunts: `budget = 150` pipelines per cell (≈ 2.6 s at 17 ms; ≈ 25 cells ≈ 65 s worst case, OFF-GATE); exhaustion is typed `exhausted` with `attempted` — never a throw, never a silent drop. **Output** `WideningProposal[]` (§1.8) + a DRAFT shift record in the door's own schema (`action: 'enroll'`, `predictedRows: 525 + k`, `proofForm: 'settlement-hash'`) to stdout/`--out`; **it writes NOTHING under the repository**. The seat pastes the proposal into the ODQ row; the owner fills `ownerWords/ownerDate/odqRow`; the enroll act appends the rows, re-records the manifest THROUGH THE DOOR (`GOLDEN_SHIFT_SIGNED=docs/shift-records/<file>.json UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 …`), then the census ritual runs downstream and strikes the exemptions the rows reached.

**Determinism, dormancy and darkness — the bit claims made and not made.** The census MOVES NOTHING (the golden's byte-identity over all 525 rows on the same gate run is the executed proof — `drift.toEqual([])` at `:781-789`); it carries NO door of its own (an instrument; its ONE dial is `BAND_FLOOR_ROWS`, a draft the owner signs); the widening is the output-moving act and it is owner-signed by standing law (§759.4; GOLDEN §1.2 "Widening waits for the freeze act by construction"); same-seed determinism across paths is WORKER's bit claim, consumed here (§1.12) and offered back as a 525-row byte-identity control corpus.

### §6.3 Laws honoured
THE PROMISE → the census moves nothing; the widening is the ONLY output-moving act and it is an `enroll` through the door, owner-signed (the GOLDEN register's `_doc`: `enroll` is chair-signed vetoably for a NEW instrument, but "new rows in your signed surfaces stay yours (§759.4)" — the golden's rows are the owner's, so widening rows are owner-signed even though the verb is `enroll`); the draft record's `predictedRows: 525 + k` is the §858 predicted-then-held discipline made a field (door refusal 4, PREDICTION_MISS). DARK BY CONSTRUCTION → no dormancy of its own; the `dial-gated` exemption for `densityRungRole` CONSUMES D4's bit claim (§1.5) rather than re-arguing it; determinism across paths is consumed from WORKER (§1.12). FINITE SEMANTICS → the denominator derives from closed vocabularies; every finding is a `CENSUS_REASONS` word; every exemption reason and proposal kind is a TOTAL positive predicate; the pinned lists are execution-checked claims (J1) and COV-7 asks to promote them. THE NEWS ADDRESS LAW → every `WideningRow` carries `cures · reason · ownerRow · proposedBy · proposedAtSha`; every refusal prints `{ reason, field, band, detail }`; the refreeze prints old→new PER BAND. THE ENGINE CEILING → zero src bytes; J1 refuses five exports on the 236 B margin. THE INSTRUMENT ESTATE → Car 0 predicts every count before Car 1 measures; nine in-memory plants run every gate; build-efficiency §7 → the executed arm runs UNCONDITIONALLY (the refreeze is an opt-in ARM of the same test, not a tier) and its ≈ 9 s solo / ≈ 60 s parallel cost is stated (§6.7 R1 prices the fallback); the `mechanism` mint-check reads through `codeOnly` keeping strings. SEQUENCING → §6.6. OWNER-GATED → the golden manifest's KEY SHAPE is a persisted shape (`key-extension` is handed to the owner, COV-3), `BAND_FLOOR_ROWS` is signed not chosen (COV-1), the threat re-key moves 523 rows' keys and hashes (COV-2).

## §7 WRWALKER — the writer-without-reader walker and its DARK register

### §7.0 Thesis
The reader-with-no-writer walker (OSR) already runs the real producers and folds every written key into `corpus.shapes` keyed `"<key> on <leafShape>"`; WRWALKER is its dual and consumes that same object unchanged, so the two instruments share ONE corpus builder and ONE identity grammar (⟦A26 E10⟧ — not 'one executed truth': they are one builder and TWO executions, OSR's `beforeAll` and WRWALKER's, and the `corpusMeta` pin holds nine COUNTS equal, which two runs can satisfy while a shape's key set differs; so WRWALKER ALSO freezes `shapesDigest = sha256(canonical(corpus.shapes))` and asserts it against a digest of the same object computed in-process, making cross-process determinism a BIT — or, when the deferred `globalSetup` corpus cache lands, there is one execution and the digest is the cache's; ⟦G0-6⟧ CONFIRMED at C′ by Car 0: the nine `corpusMeta` counts EQUAL and the live meta carries NO tenth field, AND fifteen independent processes reproduced ONE verdict digest — so the `shapesDigest` pin is cheap and will hold, and is still the right pin because counts-equal is not shapes-equal). For every written identity on a judgeable shape it asks one static question — is this key READ in a module reachable by import from a customer-facing renderer root (web components live from `src/main.jsx`, the dossier PDF, the campaign PDF, the World Book, Foundry), where reachability follows static imports, `import()` and `new URL(…, import.meta.url)` edges, resolves extensionless specifiers, and STOPS at the engine boundary — and grades the answer `R` (the OSR resolver grounds the receiver to that shape), `N` (a name-level read of `.key` on an unresolvable receiver — the conservative floor the resolver's 7.7 % reach forces), or nothing. An identity with no `R`/`N` read in any counting surface class is DARK, and a dark identity must be either a row in a hand-authored, typed, live-convicted DARK register (§1.10) or a member of a shrink-only banked cohort frozen at genesis — otherwise "generated and silently unshown" is a red with ceiling 0. Zero `src/` bytes; two hooks the rest of the horizon consumes: the per-surface-class reach map (CHARSET's renderer question) and the `pending-surface` backlog (READERREVIEW's starting list). Two recon figures moved under the lane's own read and are corrected: `PUBLIC_TOPLEVEL_KEYS` has **38** members (not 37; ⟦G0-7⟧ CONFIRMED at C′ — 38, `NPC_PUBLIC_KEYS` 11, the 49 allowlisted identities graded `json-export = A`, none LIT on that arm alone); OSR's `resolveSpec` cannot follow an **extensionless** specifier, which the 25 lazy tabs all use — load-bearing for the closure. Landing base = the COUPLED tip (rows 1–2 and the culture strike exist only there — `a-cure-site-lives-at-the-coupled-tip`). ⟦G0-6⟧ **Car 0 (receipt `$SP/laneG0WRW-receipt.md`, 04:27–04:58 ET at C′, zero bytes, every exit 0) HELD WITH ONE STOP:** the population is EXACT (`|W|` 6,520 over 337 usable shapes, 1,265 distinct written keys, 1,299 shapes), the closures are real (the extensionless `import('../new/tabs/FaithTab.jsx')` edge through `dossierLazyTabs.js` IS followed — ODQ §880.5's blindness cured by the five-suffix resolution; `pdfRender.worker.js` reaches `src/pdf/SettlementPDF.jsx`), the one-truth pin and the identity pin hold, the verdict digest is reproducible across fifteen processes, four non-vacuity controls prove the detector can say both words — and the `web` class swallowed 87 % of the pulse, which §882.3 cured by the split in §1.10 (⟦G0-2⟧, ⟦G0-9⟧). Every figure below marked ⟦G0-n⟧ is Car 0's or the fold's re-derivation; unmarked figures are the design's and stand.

### §7.1 The mechanism
**The write census — one object, borrowed whole.** `buildObservedCorpus()` from `scripts/lib/observed-shape-corpus.mjs:726` (CONFIRMED-by-lane: producer 1 is THE GENERATION ENTRY over `CONFIGS`×`SEEDS` at `:786-790` — 4×4; producers 2–3 the pulse and the steading mint; returns `{ shapes, arrayShapes, singleHome, graph, rootShapes, meta }`); WRWALKER calls it in its own `beforeAll` and never edits it (editing any of the eleven `scannerToolFiles()` is an OSR schema-16 migration). **The judged population W**: `known = { s ∈ shapes : rows ≥ MIN_ROWS }` with `MIN_ROWS = 40` imported from `observed-shape-baseline.mjs:273` — the same floor `makeResolver` uses, so write and read sides judge ONE population; `W = { (k, s) : s ∈ known, k ∈ shapes[s].keys }`; `writerIdentity(k, s)` pinned EQUAL to OSR's `identityOf` by an executed test (§1.3); at the tip `sentinel.totalKeys 6520` over `usableShapes 337` (CONFIRMED (recon); ⟦G0-7⟧ CONFIRMED EXACT at C′ by Car 0 — 6,520 over 337, 1,265 distinct keys) — W is 6,520 pairs; THIN shapes are reported, never judged (9,142 keys on 962 thin shapes at C′). **One-truth pin**: the frozen register's `corpusMeta` EQUALS OSR's register's (`generations 16, seeds 4, pulseIntervals 12, steadingsMinted 12, simulationFlagsLit 79, shapeCount 1299, originCount 8558, transitionCount 14490` — plus `configs 4`, the ninth field the volume's list omitted — at `60255ca8e`; ⟦G0-7⟧ Car 0 at C′: all nine EQUAL to the register frozen at `c28c7b43…` (`configs 4 · generations 16 · originCount 8558 · pulseIntervals 12 · seeds 4 · shapeCount 1299 · simulationFlagsLit 79 · steadingsMinted 12 · transitionCount 14490`) and the live meta carries NO tenth field; OSR's own `sentinel {resolvedReads 9420, totalKeys 6520, usableShapes 337}`, the live scan resolving 9,425 of 122,969 reads = **7.67 %** — the recon's 7.7 % reach CONFIRMED) ⟦A26 E10⟧ AND the frozen `shapesDigest` equals the in-process digest of `corpus.shapes` (counts equal is not shapes equal); a landing that moves OSR's corpus reds both registers until both re-freeze (§7.7 R6).

**The surface classes — §1.10's closed vocabulary and a two-phase closure.** `web`: phase A `LIVE = reach(main.jsx, ALL edges, no stop) ∩ src/components/**` (566 files under `src/components` at the tip → ⟦G0-8⟧ **565** at C′, LIVE 544, orphans 21; orphans fall out), phase B `reach(LIVE, ALL edges, STOP)`, ⟦G0-2⟧ then PARTITIONED: `web-display = closure(web) ∩ WEB_DISPLAY_DIRS` (679 at C′, THE COUNTING CLASS) and `web-transitive = closure(web) \ web-display` (883, `countsForLit: false`); `dossier-pdf`/`campaign-pdf`/`world-book`/`foundry`: `reach(roots, ALL, STOP)` over §1.10's roots; `json-export`: `src/lib/worldExport.js` + the allowlist arm, **`countsForLit: false`** (the export CONTROL is not wired — `worldExport.js:34-41` "a deliberate deferred follow-on"); `news`: a TAG over the web closure by `NEWS_TAG_PATTERNS` (`region/wizardNews.js`, `worldPulse/*News*.js`, `worldPulse/eventProse.js`, `worldPulse/chronicle.js`, `lib/chronicle.js`, `display/news*.js`, `components/map/herald*.js`).

> ⟦G0-9⟧⟦CHAIR §882.3⟧ **The STOP judgment was RE-OPENED by Car 0's STOP and RE-CLOSED by the split, not by a new STOP set.** Measured, the cost was not a leak: **380 of 437 `src/domain/worldPulse` files (86.96 %) inside `web`** — the kernel almost whole — which with the deliberately over-permissive `N` credit and the 7.67 % resolver reach is why **77.36 %** of the population read LIT-NAME. The lane priced three options and took none (rewriting the STOP set is the design act §7.5 says to STOP for): (1) keep the closure and red on `R` only — refuted by F3, ⟦G0-14⟧; (2) split `web` into a counting display class and a reported transitive class; (3) STOP at `src/domain/worldPulse/**` after all and cure the false DARKs with `engine-internal` rows — 287 dark identities by writer dir under `web` whole, 700 under the split, so 'by the hundred' is right in magnitude. The lane recommended (2) vetoably; the chair ADOPTED (2) PROVISIONALLY (§882.3). **The STOP SET ITSELF IS UNCHANGED** (`src/generators/`, `src/store/`, `src/workers/`, `src/lib/instantWorld/` — declared, tested, plantable, and load-bearing at the file level: control 3); what changed is which part of the web closure COUNTS. Under the split no `worldPulse` file is under a display dir by construction, so the leak-through is a REPORTED figure of `web-transitive` and cannot fire; the STOP that survives is the population one — `dark > 60 %` of judged — which the split passes at 20.34 % (fold re-derivation). **JUDGMENT (fold): the §7.5 Car 0 STOP 'kernel leak-through > 50 %' is RETIRED as satisfied-by-construction under the split and replaced by a REPORT line (`web-transitive` carries N of 437 pulse files) — a rule that cannot fire is not a rule — vetoable; veto shape: keep it as a STOP over `web-transitive` and it fires forever at 87 %.**

**The edge kinds**, extracted per parsed source with the TypeScript AST `buildIndex` already holds: (1) `ImportDeclaration` with a string specifier; (2) `ExportDeclaration` with `moduleSpecifier`; (3) `CallExpression` whose callee is `ImportKeyword` with a string-literal argument (`lazy(() => import('../new/tabs/FaithTab.jsx'))`); (4) `new URL('<spec>', import.meta.url)` — the worker construction edge (`generateSettlementPDF.js:93-94`); (5) `require('<spec>')`. Only RELATIVE specifiers into `src/` are edges. Resolution tries the literal path, `+'.js'`, `+'.jsx'`, `+'/index.js'`, `+'/index.jsx'` — OSR's `resolveSpec` (`legacy-reader-shape-scan.mjs:148-152`) resolves the literal only, and the 25 lazy tabs are extensionless (`dossierLazyTabs.js:13-66`) — exactly the blindness the TAIL-F rehearsal recorded (memory: *a reachability matcher must see `import()` and `.jsx`*; ODQ §880.5). A plant proves each edge kind is followed.

**The read census — a sibling scanner over OSR's resolver, unchanged.** `scanSurfaceReads({ files, corpus, minRows, root, closures, relOverride })` in `scripts/lib/writer-reach-scan.mjs`: `idx = buildIndex(files)`, `{ resolve, known } = makeResolver(idx, corpus.shapes, corpus.arrayShapes, minRows, corpus.singleHome, corpus.rootShapes)` — both imported unchanged (`:124`, `:257`); for every file inside at least one class closure, walk the AST and record READ SITES of a key: `PropertyAccessExpression` (not a write target — `isWriteTarget` is module-private at `:516`, so the lib carries a ten-line local twin pinned against `=`, compound assignment, `delete`, `++/--`), `ElementAccessExpression` with a string literal, `ObjectBindingPattern` elements (the destructuring form OSR does not count — `scanReaders` visits `PropertyAccessExpression` only, `:542`), `'k' in x`; a local `BUILTIN_MEMBERS` (OSR's is module-private at `legacy-reader-shape-scan.mjs:67`) — ⟦G0-10⟧ a BYTE-EXACT COPY of OSR's **72** names, pinned EQUAL by a test that extracts OSR's literal from its source TEXT (a text read of a governed file is not an edit), with a second arm asserting no key in W collides with it (OSR's set ∩ the 1,265 written keys = ∅ at C′, fold-measured). Why the pin is load-bearing: Car 0's probe carried a 75-name hand twin that ADDED `current exec from name of test toReversed toSorted` and OMITTED `findLast findLastIndex localeCompare matchAll normalize`, and three of its additions — **`name`, `from`, `test` — are WRITTEN keys**, so every `.name` property read in 1,563 files was suppressed; the fold re-ran the probe with OSR's exact set: the tally moves 596 / 5,044 / 880 → **608 / 5,033 / 879** (twelve identities `N`→`R`; `test on root` DARK→lit; the 124 `name on <shape>` identities read 113 LIT-NAME · 11 LIT). Output `reads: Map<identity, { R: Set<relPath>, N: Set<relPath> }>` + `stats`.

**The verdict, the class map, the export arm** (§1.10 `WriterVerdict`): `reach[c] = 'R'` if some `R` reader ∈ closure(c), else `'N'` if some `N` reader ∈ closure(c); the `news` tag applies the same rule over the tagged subset; the allowlist arm — `settlement` × `PUBLIC_TOPLEVEL_KEYS` (38, `publicSafe.js:52-61`) and `npcs` × `NPC_PUBLIC_KEYS` (11, `:113-116`) → `reach['json-export'] = 'A'`, constants IMPORTED, never regexed. `verdict`: `THIN` if `rows < minRows`; `LIT` if any COUNTING class has `'R'`; `LIT-NAME` if only `'N'`; else `DARK` (⟦G0-2⟧ counting = `COUNTING_CLASSES`; `web-transitive` and `json-export` are computed into `reach` and REPORTED, never counted).

No `UPDATE_*` env spelling anywhere (GOLDEN's Arm 2 censuses `process.env.UPDATE_[A-Z_]*`).

> **JUDGMENT (WRWALKER): CLI flags over an env ritual, OSR's own spelling — vetoable.**

Zero `package.json` bytes: the gate authority is the vitest walker (OSR's `check:observed-shape-readers` is likewise NOT in the `check` chain); build-efficiency §7 honoured — no chain step, no conditional arm.

**The lit-dial arm (Car 3) — the generation-side dormancy fence WRWALKER contributes.** `scripts/lib/writer-reach-lit-corpus.mjs` `buildLitDialCorpus({ configs = CONFIGS, seeds = SEEDS })`: for each `cfg × seed` run `generateSettlementPipeline({ ...cfg, [DENSITY_LAW_CONFIG_KEY]: REGISTER_VII_DENSITY_LAW_VERSION, [LIVING_CONTENT_LAW_CONFIG_KEY]: ROSTER_LIVING_CONTENT_LAW_VERSION }, null, { seed, customContent: eligibleCustomContent(REFERENCE_PACK, { tier: cfg.settType }) })` and fold the 16 roots with `foldCorpus(roots)` (`observed-shape-corpus.mjs:519`) → `litShapes`; dials and the pack IMPORTED (a fixture import from `scripts/lib` has OSR's precedent, `spatialPackFixtures.js`). `dialGated = { (k,s) ∈ W_lit : (k,s) ∉ W_dark, s ∈ known_lit }`. **Law:** every `dialGated` identity carries a `generation-dial` row, and every `generation-dial` row's identity is in `dialGated` — set equality both ways, an executed dormancy bit (present lit, absent dark) — the analogue of `discoverSimulationFlags` for the generation-side dial family OSR's corpus never lights (0 hits for `_densityLawVersion` in the corpus builder). Cost: 16 generations ≈ 464 ms + one fold. **The MAT retro-control, EXECUTED at the landing base:** `customContentRoster on settlement ∈ dialGated` — at `60255ca8e` the key does not exist; from the MAT pick onward it is written, gated and unread — a textbook writer-without-reader WRWALKER reports RED unless row 1 is present (⚠ at the BASE the convicting instrument was the presentation-claim blind arm, not this dual — the design does not claim otherwise).

### §7.3 Laws honoured
THE PROMISE → no `src/` byte; the producers are called read-only; the lit-dial arm passes explicit dials on its OWN in-memory configs and never flips `NEW_SETTLEMENT_*` (the corpus builder's own EP-1 note — "re-rolling a fixed reference world silently is precisely what THE PROMISE forbids" — is why the arm builds a SEPARATE corpus); golden 0/525 is the landing's bit. DARK BY CONSTRUCTION / a dormancy claim is a BIT claim → every `dark-by-construction` row is convicted by an executed bit (M13's four clauses; `dial !== lit` + present-lit/absent-dark); "A row may not claim a gate no reader can find; spell it by name or retire the row" reused verbatim. DETERMINISM as a bit claim → the corpus is seeded and `corpusMeta` pinned equal across the two walkers; a `--report` on an unchanged tree reproduces `population`/`surfaceReach` byte-for-byte. FINITE SEMANTICS → `SURFACE_CLASSES`, `ReachGrade`, `DARK_REASONS`, `door.kind` closed; `why`/`lighting` prose REQUIRED but never a fact the machine reads. THE NEWS ADDRESS LAW → the `news` tag lets READERREVIEW ask, for every key a news module reads, whether it reaches the Herald; WRWALKER supplies the reach, READERREVIEW judges the address. THE ENGINE CEILING → none. THE INSTRUMENT ESTATE → predict-then-MEASURE by the standing probe; shrink-only over a frozen roster with non-vacuity plants; clause E reads CODE through `codeOnly`; OSR's own laws copied (`scansRun === 2` "share the scan — do NOT raise a timeout"; the content-addressed inventory's ceiling 0 for a NEW identity; "A walker born red is a walker someone disables" — the genesis banks the cohort so the walker lands GREEN). THE CITATION LAW → clause E strips comments AND strings (a consumer's USE); clause D-flag keeps strings (the flag token IS the evidence — M13's clause 1 stays on raw bytes). THE EACH-FAMILY PARK CEILING → no `it.each`/`describe.each`/loop-generated titles. SEQUENCING → moves nothing. OWNER-GATED → the genesis bank is chair-signed vetoably; every `pending-surface` row is an owed CAR and a capability question; `json-export` counting is wiring a feature; the cohort's triage is product taste (§7.6).

Product taste ("never present a cheaper option as the best available") — the lane DRAFTS the partition by root shape with evidence (`readers.N` files, writer path, rows); the owner rules (WRW-2). Lands during the exhaustive review, before the soak. Proof: every conversion is a `--write` shrink with `registerDigest` moving in the same commit; the walker green at every step. ## §8 CHARSET — compile-time charset validation, data-driven from renderer coverage

### §8.0 Thesis
Custom content is validated for shape and length by ONE generated projection consumed at four walls (client compact, client rich, edge, DB), and not one of them asks whether a codepoint can be DRAWN — so the estate's own reference pack, mutated by one CJK letter, prints a `q` in a paid dossier and a blank in a paid World Book (the mechanism CONFIRMED-by-lane in GLYPH's docblock, `renderedFontEmbedding.test.js:22-35`, in COUPLED). The cure is a **charset wall at COMPILE**: a zero-import lazy leaf `src/domain/content/customContentCharset.js` that, for every free-text field, checks each codepoint against the ALLOWED SET OF THE SURFACES THAT FIELD REACHES, where every allowed set is DERIVED by the existing manifest compiler from the renderers' own bytes (§1.11: 759 · 218 · **190** — ⟦G0-20⟧ CHARSET Car 0 CONFIRMED 759 and 218 to the byte and measured `S` = 190 under the context-anchored reading, ⟦G0-3⟧), emitted as a fifth generated artifact (+ its edge twin), byte-checked in the gate's existing `validate:custom-content-manifest` step so a font swap, a jsPDF bump or a sanitiser edit reds the gate instead of silently moving the paid surface. Rejections are TYPED (six codes), carry a fix hint through `t()`, are shown beside the field — never a silent strip. The wall ships behind a typed door in the manifest (`charsetPolicy.enforcement: report`), its allowed sets are a FUNCTION of the owner's non-Latin decision (`nonLatin: undecided`), it never refuses content that already exists (THE CHOKEPOINT LAW, §1.11), the shipped name pools get the same check as a data lint (0 dossier misses; 41 campaign-book misses frozen as the transliteration debt), and the whole thing lands ZERO eager bytes — measured, not predicted. **One finding the recon did not have, measured at the dock's dist (CONFIRMED-by-lane):** the compact validator AND its generated projection ride the EAGER entry chunk (`index-3lB-vvzF.js`, 576,060 B, carrying `unregistered_bucket`, `missing_required_field`, `invalid_system_field`, `bindingHash`); `campaignContentBinding.js` (a static importer, `:29`) is eager too; every OTHER custom-content module is lazy (`customContentCommands-*` 6,184 B, `customContentSliceRuntime-*` 27,608 B, `contentVocabulary-*` 48,802 B / `CompendiumPanel-*`, `custom-schema-*` 10,427 B, `generateCampaignPDF-*` 17,100 B, `generateWorldBook-*` 14,086 B, `src/copy/en.js` lazy). **Consequence for every section below: not one byte of charset machinery may enter `customContentAdmission.js`, its generated projection, or `campaignContentBinding.js`.**

### §8.1 The mechanism
**Closed vocabularies** (§1.11): `CharsetSurface` (six = WRWALKER's eight minus `news` and `web-transitive`, `web` spelled `web-display` — ⟦G0-2⟧), `CharsetRejectionCode` (six, snake_case per the house idiom), `CharsetEnforcement = 'report'|'refuse'` (THE DOOR, dark = `report`), `NonLatinPolicy = 'undecided'|'embed'|'transliterate'|'keep_and_mark'` (THE OWNER'S ROW as data). **Data shapes**: `RangeString` (an ordered list of inclusive ranges in one hex string, decoded once into a `Uint8Array` BMP bitmap (8 KB) + a sorted astral range list — never a regex literal); `SurfaceCharset { ranges, count (an actual out of derivation, never typed), method, inputs[], inputsSha256 (the vendor-bump tripwire) }`; `CharsetBans { control (C0 minus TAB/LF/CR for multiline classes, DEL, C1), bidi (U+061C, U+200E-200F, U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069), invisible (⟦G0-3⟧⟦CHAIR §882.4⟧ **U+00A0** — the NBSP both jsPDF passes silently collapse to a space, measured by Car 0 probe 3 — U+00AD, U+180E, U+200B-200D, U+2060-2064, U+FEFF, U+FFF9-FFFB), noncharacter (U+FDD0-FDEF, U+FFFE-FFFF, the 32 plane-tail nonchars) }` applied on EVERY surface including web; `FieldCharsetClass { surfaces[] (hand-declared in the manifest, cross-checked by WRWALKER), multiline (schema `text`/`shortText` only), maxCodepoints (the schema's maxLength re-stated in CODEPOINTS), osrIdentity }`; `CustomContentCharsetTable` (§1.11; 41 free-text fields at 2026-08-02.1); `CharsetRejection` (§1.11; `index` is the codepoint index, `hint` a COPY KEY, never prose). **The arithmetic (all derived, none typed)** — §1.11: `dossier-pdf` = ∩ `fontkit.characterSet` over the faces `theme.js:90-118` registers (Lora ×4, Nunito ×4 — the `src:` literals ARE the registration, read from code with comments stripped: the citation law's mint side) = **759** (CONFIRMED (recon) via `laneTOFU-fontcov.log`; the roster CONFIRMED-by-lane); `campaign-pdf`/`world-book` = `C = {20..7E} ∪ {A0..FF} ∪ W`, `W = keys(new jsPDF().getFont().metadata.Unicode.encoding.WinAnsiEncoding)` (the runtime table the encoder reads, `jspdf.es.js:3011-3027`; the 27-entry map's own source comment at `:21116` CONFIRMED-by-lane; ~~the runtime PATH is PLAUSIBLE — Car 0 probe 1 settles it, fallback §8.7 R1~~ ⟦G0-20⟧ **the runtime PATH is CONFIRMED** — CHARSET Car 0 probe 1 on jspdf 4.2.1 in the dock: `metadata.Unicode` present, Helvetica bound to `WinAnsiEncoding`, `|W|` **27** with `U+20AC → 128`, `W ∩ ({20..7E} ∪ {A0..FF}) = ∅`, and the recon's decode of the dependency's own compressed block yields the SAME 27 pairs byte for byte (`CROSS-ASSERT A === B: true`) — §8.7 R1 RETIRED, §14 E27 SETTLED) = **218** (CONFIRMED: 95 + 96 + 27; `|D ∩ C|` 217, `C \ D = {U+00AD}`); `S = { cp ∈ C : sanitizeJsPdfText('a' + String.fromCodePoint(cp) + 'a') === 'a' + String.fromCodePoint(cp) + 'a' }` — ⟦G0-3⟧⟦G0-20⟧ the CONTEXT-ANCHORED whole pass, MEASURED by execution = **190** today (volume 191 → measured 190; the bare single-codepoint predicate the volume wrote reads 189 because `' '.trim()` is empty; `s()` strips all 27 of `W` — its class is `\x20-\x7E\xA0-\xFF` — and its `\s+` collapse turns `U+00A0` into `U+0020`, which is why NBSP is now banned); `web`/`foundry`/`json-export` `method: 'unbounded'` — the bans still apply; a field reaching several surfaces = the intersection minus the bans (`institutions.name` = `D ∩ S` = **189** (volume 190 → 189); `U+00AD` is in `S`, not in `D`, and banned anyway; `U+00A0` is in `D`, not in `S`, and banned now too — ⟦G0-3⟧). **Under the owner's decision the derivation re-parameterises WITHOUT code change**: `embed` + `embeddedFont: 'public/fonts/<file>.ttf'` ⇒ `campaign-pdf`/`world-book` become `fontkit-single` over that TTF (∩ `S` until Car 3, after which `S == C`); `transliterate` ⇒ sets unchanged, `transliterationPreview()` wired into the rejection detail; `keep_and_mark` ⇒ sets unchanged, an `uncovered_codepoint` on a PDF surface becomes `accepted_with_mark` (a SEPARATE `marks[]` list; the verdict changes, the vocabulary does not).

**The protocol at an authoring chokepoint, in order:** (1) type/length shape admission runs first and unchanged (the compact wall, eager, untouched); (2) `validateCustomContentCharset(bucket, definition, table)` runs ONLY at the AUTHORING chokepoints (§1.11 THE CHOKEPOINT LAW) — NEVER at hydration, campaign binding, archive restore, or ⟦A6 P1 · A32 O1⟧ own-export ACCOUNT IMPORT (a RESTORE path: the adapter is called with `authoring: false`, charset findings become per-entry `charset_legacy` marks, the pack is never skipped whole for a charset reason); (3) per free-text field, per string (list items individually): a lone surrogate → `malformed_encoding` and stop for that string; codepoints counted, `> maxCodepoints` → `length`; per codepoint: `bans.control` (unless TAB/LF/CR and `multiline`) → `control_character`; `bans.bidi` → `bidi_override`; `bans.invisible`/`noncharacter` → `invisible_format`; else for each of the field's surfaces in declared order, a bounded set lacking it → `uncovered_codepoint` with `surface` = the FIRST such surface and `surfaces` = all; one rejection per offending codepoint OCCURRENCE (the author sees each by position); `nfcWouldPass` computed once per string by re-running on `value.normalize('NFC')`; (4) under `report` the admission `ok` is UNCHANGED and the list rides beside (`charset: CharsetRejection[]`); under `refuse` a non-empty list makes `ok: false` and the rejections append to `errors` in the house shape; the store keeps the legacy `customContentError` string (unchanged spelling) AND a new transient `customContentCharsetRejections` (never persisted — Car 2 proof 4); (5) the author sees the rejection beside the field (`CustomContentEditor.jsx` `EditorField`, the `FIELD_HINTS` slot `:389-398`) rendered from `t(hint, { char, position, surfaceName, max, actual })` — ⟦A57 L7⟧ `surfaceName` is the product NOUN from the copy map `errors.customContentCharset.surface.{dossier-pdf: 'the dossier PDF', campaign-pdf: 'the campaign book', world-book: 'the World Book', web, foundry, json-export}`, never the class token, and `char` is the glyph, never `U+5F71`: the hint reads 'The dossier PDF cannot print 影 (character 8 of the name).' — with a fix action typed by policy: `nfcWouldPass` → "Normalise" (rewrites the DRAFT only); `transliterate` → "Use ASCII preview"; otherwise the hint names the surface that cannot print it. Nothing is stripped, folded or rewritten without the author's click.

**The `length` reason and the UTF-16 drift.** The compact wall measures `value.length` (UTF-16 units); the SQL wall `char_length` (codepoints); the leaf counts CODEPOINTS (`unit: 'codepoints'`). Residual: a string of ≤160 codepoints with astral characters may still fail the compact wall as `invalid_value` — impossible for any PDF-reaching field (every astral codepoint is `uncovered_codepoint` first; `D` and `S` are BMP-only), possible for web-only fields; curing the compact wall costs ~30–40 eager bytes — **deliberately deferred and recorded (CS-7), not a bug to re-find.** **The three shapes of the owner's row exist as code paths from Car 1, lit by data:** `transliterationPreview(value)` (§1.11's map; prior art `civility.js:198-208` `foldCodePoint`, NOT `kernel/slugify.js` which deletes — `Kovačević → kova-evi`); dark by construction — exported and unit-tested from Car 1, wired into the detail only under `transliterate`; a dormancy control asserts no `asciiPreview` key under `undecided`; `accepted_with_mark` lands the TYPE never the placeholder (paid-surface bytes, the owner's); `embed` is the derivation's `fontkit-single` method — the jsPDF font-embedding itself is a CAPABILITY (the chair's own words, `TUNING-DESK.md:568`) and not this item's.

**The door and the register** (§1.4): `schema/custom-content.manifest.json` gains `"charsetPolicy": { "enforcement": "report", "nonLatin": "undecided", "embeddedFont": null }`, validated by the compiler against the closed vocabularies (fail closed); lighting `report → refuse` is a one-line manifest edit + `npm run gen:custom-content-manifest` + a ledger row (Car 2's recorded act); dormancy is a BIT claim — every existing admission suite passes byte-identical under `report` plus a control that the rich adapter's `ok` is unchanged on a hostile string. THE REGISTER is the generated table, byte-checked by `--check` in gate step 5 (`validate:custom-content-manifest`, `package.json:14,58`) — no new npm script (⛔ `package.json` is one of OSR's eleven scanner-tool files; a byte there is a governed schema migration) and no new chain step (build-efficiency §7; `ciCheckParity` pins the chain against the five groups); the derivation costs the existing step ~1 s. **The data-lint register**: `tests/data/namingDataCharset.test.js` (§1.11 THE TRANSLITERATION-DEBT REGISTER) — growth reds; shrinkage demands a re-record (the exact-equality idiom); because `namingDecontamination.test.js:47-56` pins pool SIZES for rng draw stability, a transliteration of the pools is a same-seed shift only if it changes length/order, which the two pins together make impossible to do silently.

### §8.3 Laws honoured
THE PROMISE → the wall runs at AUTHORING chokepoints only; hydration/binding/restore charset-blind by law AND by build pin; Cars 1–2 same-seed-neutral (the 525 golden + the dormancy fences asserted unchanged as a BIT claim in Car 1 proof 2); Car 3 DOES move paid-surface bytes (dashes, curly quotes, `Uroš`, `Hadžić` start printing) and is sequenced BEFORE the GOLDEN freeze with its declaration as MACHINERY (⟦A8 P3 · A45 O14⟧ the debt register's re-record, the painted-text pin, the ODQ row CS-3 — never a `docs/shift-records/*` file while the register is UNFROZEN) and an owner veto row; it moves no golden (no painter-byte golden exists — `worldBook.test.js:4-6` "never bytes, never the painter"). DARK BY CONSTRUCTION → `charsetPolicy.enforcement` is the typed door at `report`; dormancy is the byte-identical pass of every admission suite + the `ok`-unchanged control; lighting is a recorded manifest edit; determinism — the leaf is pure and the client/edge parity arm proves the SAME verdict on both paths (WORKER's one-code-path law honoured for the wall). FINITE SEMANTICS → six codes, six surfaces, two policy vocabularies, hints as COPY KEYS; the clerk's `CONTENT_UNSUPPORTED_REASONS` grows by the same six so the wall's verdict on a model's output is a typed fact the repair loop acts on. THE NEWS ADDRESS LAW → the rejection shown is `field · uncovered_codepoint · the World Book cannot print 影 (character 8 of the name)` — actor, typed action, the SURFACE as the reason in a product noun with the glyph itself (⟦A57 L7⟧ never `U+5F71`, never `dossier-pdf`); addressable (Normalise / ASCII preview / edit); a store test pins that the sync banner never contains `U+` or a surface class token. THE ENGINE CEILING → every byte's chunk named; the leaf's only law-bound edge is FROM lazy content modules; eager bytes measured 0 by the eight-chunk identity. THE INSTRUMENT ESTATE → the generated table is the register and `--check` its measurement; the face roster is read with comments stripped and the string literal kept (a MINT); the build pin's dependency claim uses `codeOnly` (comments AND strings blanked) with a plant; bills per car in §8.5. THE TWO PDF RENDERERS → two DIFFERENT derived sets, a per-field surface list, the owner's three shapes as data re-parameterising the derivation without code (the owner's row NOT pre-empted; the paid surface safe under any of the three). SEQUENCING → Cars 1–2 instruments at any slot; Car 3 before GOLDEN. OWNER-GATED → Car 4 inert until the owner names its call site; jsPDF font-embedding is a CAPABILITY; the non-Latin decision untouched; `report → refuse` changes paid-surface AUTHORING acceptance and, until CS-9's restore path lands, RESTORE acceptance too — so it is HELD for the LIGHTING WAVE after CS-9, WITH a veto row (CS-2) (⟦A6 P1 · A32 O1⟧⟦CHAIR-R9⟧). ## §9 WORKER — generation in a Web Worker so the tab never freezes; ONE code path, a typed protocol, the store boundary async

### §9.0 Thesis
Generation already runs behind an `async` store action and is already worker-loadable — `customContentPreview.worker.js` runs `generateSettlementPipeline` in a production Web Worker today — so the WORKER item is not "make generation async" but **move the generation LANE out of the eager store slice into a lazy module, and give that module one transport-neutral core that the Web Worker and the in-thread fallback both call** (§1.12). The core takes a plain-data request and an `emit`, runs the pipeline with the same `onStep` summaries the slice computes today, applies the locked-roster carry, and returns plain data; the shell is 40 lines of `self.onmessage`; the client is the advance-worker transport idiom (one worker per request, per-STEP liveness watchdog, transport failure → in-thread once, pipeline throw → reject, `AbortSignal` → terminate). The protocol is typed and closed; the shape that crosses is the persisted settlement shape whose schema law already forbids function-bearing members; the in-thread path clones its inputs exactly as `postMessage` would — a bit claim proven by a byte-identity triple over a tier×culture sample, by a Node `worker_threads` adapter driving the SAME worker module the browser runs (the SOAKCHAIN/COVERAGE hook), and by golden 0/525. It ships DARK behind `generationWorker: false`; ⟦CHAIR-R9⟧ lighting moves no output and is the LIGHTING WAVE's recorded act (after the Playwright spec ⟦A11 P6⟧ is green); the owner sees it in the walk. Because the lane leaves the entry chunk, the design's first act is to MEASURE whether the extraction REDUCES the first-paint closure — the recorded lane-D deferral in `scripts/.size-baseline.json` says it must be re-proved against the byte-budget build tests. ⟦INTEGRATOR-AMENDED⟧ The design's closing clause ("if ≥ 305 B it is also the third option nobody has priced for T13's 205 B re-ask") is RETIRED by §880.8: the re-ask is CLOSED (Option A); Car 0's number is HEADROOM under the ruled ceiling and the owner's WK-10 asks what to do with it (§13 C16). The COUPLED consist touches NONE of the files this design edits (CONFIRMED-by-lane at `1f60b8599`) — only the lighting tuple, the closure ceiling and the `__vite__mapDeps` manifest are shared.

### §9.1 The mechanism
**What moves and what stays.** The generate action (`settlementSlice.js:335-627`) is one function with five phases, cut at exactly one seam: **A. pre-flight** (tier gate `:340-346`, anon cap `:356-361`, `fullConfig = geographyLockedConfig(locks, settlement, birthConfig({...}))` `:377-390`, `await Promise.all([loadEngine(), loadSettlementContentRuntimeOptions(state)])`, `seed = seedOverride || eng.generateSeed()` `:397`) → the lane body `src/store/settlementGenerateAction.js` (lazy), verbatim, `generateSeed` imported statically from `../kernel/prng.js`; **B. THE WORK** (`eng.generateSettlementPipeline(fullConfig, neighbor, { seed, ...contentRuntimeOptions, onComplete, onStep })` `:408-426` + `eng.carryLockedRosterThroughGenerate(state.settlement, result, state.locks)` `:472`) → `runGenerationRequest(request, emit)` — THE ONE CORE, in the worker or in-thread on fallback; **C. post-steps** (tier re-gate `:443-446`, `carryLockedSections`, `reconcileSettlementChange`, `activateFaithIfEntitled(reconciled, get)`, `deriveSystemState`) → the lane body, verbatim over the cloned result, main; **D. the commit `set()`** `:496-542` → the lane body, verbatim except `state.lastCtx = null`; **E. after** (anon counter, analytics `:558-611`, generation-id spine, `return withFaith`) → the lane body. The slice keeps the action KEY on its literal (the operation-registry walker's denominator counts an action that passes `set` to a lazily-loaded helper) and delegates by the established idiom (`recordSnapshot: (opts = {}) => recordSnapshotAction(set, get, opts)`, `:286`): `generateSettlement: (seedOverride) => loadGenerateLane().then((lane) => lane.generateSettlementAction(set, get, seedOverride))`, `loadGenerateLane = () => (_laneP ||= import('./settlementGenerateAction.js'))`. `loadEngine()` shrinks to the ONE import `regenSection` still needs, so `engineChunkLazy.test.js:261-263`'s arm 1 stays green verbatim; arm 2 is RE-ANCHORED on a MUTATION (§9.2).

> **JUDGMENT (WORKER): the carry crosses INTO the core (topology T2, "the wire-lean shape") over leaving it on main (T1, "the simple shape"), because `carryLockedRosterThroughGenerate` lives in the pipeline module (`generateSettlementPipeline.js:353`) — the `engine` chunk — so on main it would still fetch the engine chunk on every first generate and the worker would ADD a bundle instead of replacing one; its inputs (`previousSettlement`, `locks`) and its report (`_preservation: { preserved[], overflow[] }`, JSDoc `:349`) are plain data; the owner's law is the best product, not the simplest patch — vetoable; the price is that a reroll's request carries the previous settlement (≈ 0.5 MB, ≈ 10 ms to clone at the soak's measured 9.9 ms/0.51 MB) and Car 0 censuses `locks`/`_preservation` for cloneability.**

**The protocol** — §1.12 (`src/lib/generationProtocol.js`, a ZERO-IMPORT leaf, the `customContentPreviewProtocol.js` idiom). The rules it encodes, each a FINITE-SEMANTICS law: no function crosses (the slice's `onStep`/`onComplete` callbacks become the `step` event and the `result` packet); no wall-clock crosses (`pipelineHistory[].ts` stamped by the RECEIVER on main at message receipt — `src/store/**` is outside every determinism ban; the worker file may not call `Date.now()` — the `src/workers/**` eslint block); no store crosses (the request carries `customContent` explicitly so the pipeline takes the per-run `withCustomContent` branch at `generateSettlementPipeline.js:137-139` and never the boot-wired global getter `store/index.js:154` — the advance worker's "dedicated protocol field" law, `advanceWorkerClient.js:27-30`); one request per worker instance; deliberately NO `cancel` packet — a synchronous pipeline cannot be interrupted by a message, only by `terminate()` (`townSceneWorkerClient.js:143-145`). Error codes: `pipeline_threw` (the in-thread path would throw identically → REJECT, never retry), `protocol_mismatch` (a stale cached worker script → REJECT; a retry hits the same script), `request_invalid`, `cancelled` (`DOMException('Generation cancelled','AbortError')`).

**The core** `runGenerationRequest(request, emit)` (`src/workers/generationRequest.js`; synchronous, pure over its inputs; no `Date`, no `Math.random`, no store, no field reads): `const { op, requestId, payload } = structuredClone(request)` — the boundary made explicit on BOTH paths; unknown op → `request_invalid`; `onStep = (name, ctx) => { meta = metaForStep(name); summary = meta.summary ? meta.summary(ctx) : null (the slice's own tolerance :420-422); pipelineHistory.push({ id: name, summary }); emit({ kind: 'generation.step', requestId, workerContract, step: { id, index, summary } }) }`; `fresh = generateSettlementPipeline(fullConfig, importedNeighbour, { seed, ...contentRuntime, onStep })`; `{ settlement, _preservation = null } = carryLockedRosterThroughGenerate(previousSettlement, fresh, locks)`; returns `{ settlement, preservation, pipelineHistory, resolvedConfig: fullConfig }`. Three pinned properties: (i) **identity with THE GENERATION ENTRY** — with `previousSettlement = null` the returned `settlement` is `toEqual` + `JSON.stringify`-equal to the direct call (the carry returns `freshSettlement` untouched when nothing is locked, `:355-361`), so the headless runners (soak, OSR corpus, golden `hashFor`, COVERAGE's census) and the worker are the SAME path by construction; (ii) **no reads** — the core never dereferences a settlement field (and `src/workers/` is in WRWALKER's STOP set — doubly safe); (iii) **the `onStep` summaries are computed where the ctx lives** — `stepMetadata.js:34` reads `ctx.generationContext.worldLaw.magicFunctions()`, a function-bearing object that could never cross, so the string is minted beside it and only the string travels.

> **JUDGMENT (WORKER): place the core under `src/workers/` so the FULL determinism ban (`determinismBanCoverage`'s `workers (sim path)` layer: `MATH_RANDOM, NEW_DATE, DATE_NOW, LOCALE_COMPARE`), T13's TREES (which include `src/workers`) and WRWALKER's STOP set all cover it by existing law, over `src/lib/` which would need a new eslint files-glob AND a new LAYERS row kept in lockstep — vetoable; the cost is one non-`*.worker.js` file in a directory that so far holds only entries, and the lazy-chunk contract names it so nobody mistakes it for a second worker.**

**The shell** `src/workers/generation.worker.js`: `self.onmessage` refuses `kind !== 'generation.request' || v !== 1 || !GENERATION_OPS.includes(op)` with `request_invalid`; runs the core with `post` on every step (tracking `lastStepId`); posts `generation.result`; catches into `generation.error { code: 'pipeline_threw', message, stepId, stack }`; every packet carries `workerContract`. No `Date`, `Math.random`, `localeCompare`, `import.meta`, DOM global.

**The client** `src/lib/generationClient.js` `runGeneration(request, { flagOn, fallback, onStep, signal, workerFactory })` (§1.12): `!flagOn → in-thread FLAG_OFF`; `typeof Worker === 'undefined' → NO_WORKER` (Node / vitest / SSR — every existing test); `signal.aborted → AbortError` before construction; factory throw/null → `CONSTRUCT_FAILED`; then a Promise with a settle-once guard: per-step watchdog `GENERATION_STEP_WATCHDOG_MS = 30_000` re-armed on every step (NOT a total-run deadline); `onmessage` ignores a late or foreign packet (`requestId` mismatch), rejects `protocol_mismatch` on a differing sentinel, relays `step`, resolves `result` with outcome `WORKER`, rejects `error` with `{ code, stepId, workerStack }`; `onerror`/`onmessageerror`/watchdog → `TRANSPORT_FAILED` → the in-thread path ONCE; `signal` → terminate + AbortError; `postMessage(request)` throwing → REJECT (an un-cloneable request means a function or class instance reached the boundary — a defect Car 0's census exists to make impossible; hiding it behind the fallback would be a false green); `terminate()` on every settle path. The taxonomy is the advance client's (`advanceWorkerClient.js:14-24`) with two deliberate departures stated: (a) the request-side `DataCloneError` rejects; (b) `protocol_mismatch` rejects rather than falls back. **The client has NO static import of the core** (T2): the body supplies `fallback = async (req, onStep) => (await loadGenerationCore()).runGenerationRequest(req, (ev) => onStep?.(ev.step))` with `loadGenerationCore = () => import('../workers/generationRequest.js')`, so on the happy path main never fetches the engine chunk.

**The store boundary — what changes and what does not.** Signature UNCHANGED: `generateSettlement(seedOverride?) → Promise<settlement|null>` was already `async`; every one of the 7 UI callers and 25 test call sites already `await`s it (recon §1.3; the two return-consumers `SettlementsPanel.jsx:146`, `GenerateWizard.jsx:226` by the recon's citations). **Resolution timing: a macrotask instead of a microtask** — the ONE observable contract change; no caller or test reads state synchronously after the call in the same tick (grep of 26 files, CONFIRMED (recon)). **Concurrency UNCHANGED by design**: two components can each pass their own latch today and both run, last `set()` wins; one worker per request preserves exactly that. **`lastCtx`: nulled on BOTH paths, field retained, deletion owner-gated** — the full ctx is function-bearing (`generationContext.js:323-345`) and cannot cross; ZERO readers in `src/` and `tests/` (`git grep lastCtx 60255ca8e -- src tests` → the slice's own 4 sites + one docblock); nulling it only on the worker path would make store state differ by transport — a fork by the law's own definition — so Car 1 writes `state.lastCtx = null` on BOTH paths and rewrites the `:173` docblock `RETIRED-DARK pending owner row WK-2` (removing the key is the owner's). **`pipelineHistory`** shape identical (`{ id, ts, summary }`; `ts` stamped on main) — ⟦A13 P8⟧ but the body takes the LIST from the RESULT packet (the core's own `pipelineHistory[{ id, summary }]`, §1.12) and stamps `ts` per row from the event receipt times it recorded, DISCARDING relayed `step` rows on a fallback: on a mid-run transport failure (a watchdog trip after step 7 of 14) the in-thread run emits every step AGAIN, and a body that pushed one row per `onStep` as today (`settlementSlice.js:412-426`) would commit 7 + 14 rows — the reveal would replay 21 steps and store state would differ by transport OUTCOME, a fork by the law's own definition; the reveal (`PipelineReveal.jsx`) replays it exactly; the owner-signed play-through (`a52a88b1`) untouched. **Anon cap naturally cancel-safe** (`incrementAnonFull/Reroll` fire only after success). **Persistence untouched and structurally kept out of the worker**: nothing under `src/workers/` may import `savesService`, `localStorage`, Supabase or the store; `engineWorkerDomFree.test.js` gains `src/workers` (and `src/generators`, `src/lib/instantWorld`) in `SCAN_DIRS` (Car 1); the generated settlement stays unpersisted (`persistProjection.js:9-22`); the protocol carries the world OUT to main and never IN to a store — the townScene law "raw world/campaign state never crosses this transport boundary" is inverted on purpose (the world IS the product), but the SAVE never crosses.

> **JUDGMENT (WORKER): keep today's concurrency semantics rather than introduce a slice-level "generating" latch or a supersede rule, because either is a NEW observable contract the owner has not asked for and the recon lists the concurrency policy as owner-gated (WK-3) — vetoable; the client's request-id correlation makes a supersede rule a ten-line follow-up.**

**The one place the in-thread path might have differed, and how the core makes both paths equal.** Today `fullConfig` is passed BY REFERENCE and read again by the analytics block (`configArchetype`, `usedRandomSentinels`, `computeConfigSignature`, `fullConfig.useCustomContent`, `_neighbourRelType` — `:574-599`); if the pipeline resolves the config IN PLACE (the instant-world composer's own comment: "the pipeline is handed an object it may resolve in place"), the analytics today see the RESOLVED config and a worker — mutating its own clone — would leave main's copy unresolved: a telemetry fork between paths. The core forecloses it on both paths: it clones the request on entry and returns `resolvedConfig` (the config AFTER the run; `fullConfig` itself post-run — exactly today's analytics input, never `settlement.config`, the §REFUTE-ME fourth attack's trap), and the body feeds analytics from it. Car 0 probe P4 measures whether the mutation exists at all AND records `JSON(fullConfigAfter) === JSON(settlement.config)` per row. `duration_ms` (analytics) stays main's wall-clock around the whole await — it now INCLUDES worker start-up and transport: a declared one-time semantic shift in a telemetry field (WK-8), named, not smuggled.

**The door** — §1.4: `generationWorker: false` in `FLAG_DEFAULTS` (`flagRegistry.js:32-70`; `simAdvanceWorker: true` at `:64` is the sibling) with a `FLAG_DESCRIPTIONS` row (`tests/lib/flags.test.js`'s every-flag-has-both invariant); read on MAIN in the body (`flag('generationWorker')`), exactly as `campaignAdvanceSession.js:447` reads `simAdvanceWorker`; OFF ⇒ the same core in-thread, byte-identical by the pin; the URL/localStorage override `?flag.generationWorker=true` lets the owner light one browser without a deploy.

**The instant world (Car 2) — the same core, a second op.** `runInstantWorld` (`instantWorldBody.js:104-276`) calls `composeInstantWorld({ seed, basicConfig, name, clock: () => now, contentRuntime })` SYNCHRONOUSLY at `:145-159`, then persists each member (main-only), then one `set()`. The design replaces the synchronous call with `await runGeneration({ …, op: 'instant-world', payload: { seed, basicConfig, name, now, contentRuntime, planLaw } }, { flagOn, fallback, onStep })` and leaves the persistence loop, the session fences (`assertSessionCurrent()` per member) and the commit untouched. Inside the core: `case 'instant-world'` builds `engine = { generateSettlementPipeline: (cfg, n, opts) => generateSettlementPipeline(cfg, n, { ...opts, onStep: memberStep }) }` and returns `composeInstantWorld({ seed, basicConfig, name, clock: () => now, contentRuntime, planLaw, engine })` — `clock` rebuilt inside the worker from the `now` STRING the store already mints (`:114`); `idFactory` left to the composer's pure default (`iw::<seed>::<n>`; main already remaps provisional ids to real save ids `:179-196`); the `engine` param is the composer's own injection point (`:209`), so no `src/lib/instantWorld` file changes and its determinism block is untouched (the composer's `mintSettlement` builds `generationOptions` with `customContent` explicit on every branch — the global getter is never consulted here either).

**The regen ops (Car 3) — the remaining two synchronous freezes.** `regenSection` (`:680-807`) runs `eng.regenNPCsPipeline(settlement, cfg, { locks })` `:731` and `eng.regenHistoryPipeline(settlement, cfg)` `:766` synchronously inside an async action; Car 3 adds ops `regen-npcs` (payload `{ settlement, config, locks }` → `{ parts, preservation }`; `generateSettlementPipeline.js:547-561`) and `regen-history` (`{ settlement, config }` → `{ history, _regenSeed }`, `:611`), moves the `regenSection` body to the lane module by the same delegation, and `loadEngine()` is then DELETED (its last importer gone) — the size-baseline row for `settlementSlice.js` lowers again or is deleted (the house test demands deletion under 800). Car 3 is droppable without harm to Cars 1–2.

**What this design deliberately does NOT do:** no live-progress UI, no Cancel button, no change to the reveal's pacing (the protocol CARRIES typed step events and the client HONOURS an `AbortSignal`; every affordance is the owner's — WK-4/WK-5); no keep-alive worker; no `vite.config.js` `manualChunks` row (none of the new modules is reached statically from an eager seed; none is a `src/domain` leaf; Rollup's default facade naming gives each its own lazy chunk — Car 0's probe build checks it before it is relied on); no idle prefetch (WK-6).

⟦G0-57⟧ **THE JUDGMENT IS PRICED AND HOLDS ON MEASUREMENT** (WORKER Car 0 receipt, `$SP/laneG0WORKER-receipt.md`) §9.5(d). The BUILT probe worker bundle (1,404,723 B) driven in Node `worker_threads` through the estate's isolate shim, town config, 5 runs, every run emitting 22 step events and 11 NPCs: **construct→ready 40.3 ms · construct→first-step 41.5 ms · construct→result 136.5 ms · ready→result 96.1 ms** (medians). Against E35's ~500 ms bound the first-step figure is **12× inside** it, and the whole cost of constructing a fresh isolate and parsing the entire 1.4 MB bundle (~40 ms) is under HALF the 96 ms generation it wraps — ⇒ **one worker per request HOLDS and the pooled/keep-alive factory follow-up is NOT OWED**. ⚠ Method stated plainly: this is not a browser; it includes thread spawn and V8 parse and EXCLUDES the network fetch, which is reported separately — on the wire the first generate additionally fetches **452,384 B gzipped** of worker bundle (the WK-6 owner posture, not a latency the isolate model changes).

> **JUDGMENT (WORKER): one worker per request (the advance idiom) over a keep-alive singleton (the PDF worker's idiom), because a resident worker holds the whole evaluated engine graph in memory between generations on every device, and the alternative's cost — construct→first-step latency — is a Car 0 figure, not a guess — vetoable; the client's `workerFactory` seam makes a pooled factory a follow-up that touches no protocol.**

### §9.3 Laws honoured
THE PROMISE → NO output moves: the worker runs the same module over cloned inputs in the same JS engine; golden 0/525 at every car's tip is the standing dormancy instrument; the two things the boundary COULD move (a `Map`/`Set`/`undefined` mangled by the clone; a config resolved in place on one path) are foreclosed by the byte pin and `resolvedConfig`; if any car's tip moves a row, that car STOPS — the shift would be undeclared and there is no door to carry it through. DARK BY CONSTRUCTION → `generationWorker: false` is the door; the bits: golden 0/525, the identity triple over 25 rows (⟦A11 P6⟧ honestly labelled: in-thread ≡ Worker-DOUBLE ≡ Node isolate — the 'worker' leg is a hand-written double running the real core in the test's own thread, and the Node isolate is the only real second engine; the browser Worker path rested on the `customContentPreview.worker` precedent, an argument), the isolate evidence (the browser worker MODULE driven headlessly ≡ the direct entry — the headless-runner leg of the clause), the BROWSER leg by ONE Playwright spec in the post-build/e2e phase (`package.json` `test:e2e` exists at `60255ca8e:50-55`; `e2e/flow-a-generate-save-export.spec.js` already exercises generate → export; §7 refuses a new chain step, so it rides the e2e phase) as the PRECONDITION of the lighting act, `lastCtx` nulled on BOTH paths so store state cannot differ by transport; the in-thread path clones its inputs so it is not "a fallback that differs". FINITE SEMANTICS → `GENERATION_OPS`, `TRANSPORT_OUTCOMES`, `GENERATION_ERROR_CODES` frozen closed; a rejection carries a `code` and a human `message` never read as a fact; `requestId` a counter; the worker-bundle band is a SANITY band and says so. THE NEWS ADDRESS LAW → the only reader-visible consequences are the reveal's `pipelineHistory` (unchanged shape) and, on failure, `errors.generateFail` — now with a typed `code` and `stepId` on the error so a future READERREVIEW fix can address "generation failed at `<step>` because `<code>`"; progress/cancel are owner rows. THE ENGINE CEILING → the core placed under `src/workers/` PRECISELY so the `/src/generators/` rule cannot route it into the 236 B margin; no `src/domain` leaf; the closure delta MEASURED in Car 0 with the replica before any product byte is committed. THE INSTRUMENT ESTATE → every tuple contribution predicted from literal counts; no `it.each`; no src/domain leaf (the four-census bill does not arise); the new src files under `src/lib`/`src/store`/`src/workers` owe zero `any` holes (`typecheck:ratchet` + `typecheck:domain:strict` are `base[file] ?? 0` for a new file), zero transcendental sites, no `hash01` caller, no size-baseline row; the re-anchored arm proven by TWO plants; no new chain step. THE OSR / WRWALKER contract → the core makes no reads and lives in the STOP set; the body under `src/store/` is STOP; neither population moves; Car 0 asserts the key census identical across the clone; `check:observed-shape-readers` at every car's tip reports the frozen inventory unchanged. SEQUENCING → moves nothing; lands dark in HORIZON-DARK; ⟦CHAIR-R9⟧ lit in the LIGHTING WAVE (not through the tuning door — no tuned value, no signature; a declared shift whose measured golden movement is ZERO by the identity triple). OWNER-GATED → nothing persisted changes; no budget raised — Car 0's STOP rule DROPS the extraction shape rather than asking; Cancel and live progress are named CAPABILITY questions; lighting is the owner's act. THE TWO PDF RENDERERS → untouched. HONESTY → the three PLAUSIBLE figures that matter (closure delta sign and size; worker-bundle wire size; construct→first-step latency) each name the Car 0 probe.
