## §10 SEQUENCING — one plan against the binding arc

**The arc (binding — ⟦CHAIR-R1⟧⟦CHAIR-R9⟧ re-cut 2026-09-02; ledger §881, §881.3, §881.4):** COUPLED+PAID (LANDED as §881 at `fab576aba`, 58 commits, gate green first run) → TAIL-F (landing NOW onto it; boards ALONE — "the fence union"; nothing rides it) → T13 (+ the mint riders; HOLDING COMPLETE at `594c7521f`, 13 commits, closure 1,047,496 / 1,048,000, 29 of 30 transcendental sites cured with ZERO golden rows moved — §881.3; nothing is inserted before it) → **HORIZON-INSTR** (the instrument-only cars: TUNEREG 1–3, SOAKCHAIN 0–4, WRWALKER 1–3; zero src bytes) → **HORIZON-DARK** (every src car, dark by construction — admitted as its OWN gate after T13 only under a BIT claim, §10.1 G5) → **THE LIGHTING WAVE** (⟦CHAIR-R9⟧ the owner's word, 2026-09-02 ~01:07 ET, verbatim: "oh light everything up before the exhaustive review." — every dark mechanism in the estate lights BEFORE the freeze as a DECLARED SHIFT; chartered by the LIGHTING-INVENTORY lane, not by this volume — §10.3) → GOLDEN freeze (L9; records the LIT world, so no per-flag door signature is owed; GOLDEN's machinery + COVERAGE Cars 1–4 on that one landing) → W-ARMS (after the freeze) → the walk + ONE regen (owner; the walk's lighting questions pre-answered YES by §881.4) → the exhaustive review+fix (owner-directed, §879.6) → the terminal soak (owner; soaks the LIT world) → the tuning signature (owner; VALUES only, still LAST) → push. **The owner's standing order (§879.6): everything is BUILT AND LANDED before the review phase.** Every HORIZON car that lands code lands BEFORE the walk; the review phase consumes instruments, it does not build them. Output-moving machinery lands BEFORE GOLDEN dark (HORIZON-DARK, a BIT claim) and is LIT in the lighting wave before the freeze as a declared shift; the GOLDEN door signs VALUES at the tuning sitting, not lightings; instruments that move nothing may land at any slot. **No HORIZON car ever rides TAIL-F's or T13's gate.**

### §10.3 What is dark at HORIZON-DARK, what the LIGHTING WAVE lights (⟦CHAIR-R9⟧), what the tuning pass signs, what the review consumes, what the terminal soak proves
⟦CHAIR-R9⟧ **Dark at HORIZON-DARK's tip (every dormancy a BIT claim, §1.4):** `demographicsEnabled` (the term, THE CROWDING LINE, THE READING — all in the tree, all `false`); `generationWorker: false` (the lane built, the worker bundle emitted, the flag off); `charsetPolicy.enforcement` at `report`; `charsetPolicy.nonLatin: undecided`; every TUNEREG row `draft` at `signatureVersion 0`; `BAND_FLOOR_ROWS` a draft; the soak register's cells UNFROZEN until the first clean run mints them; the WRWALKER cohort banked at genesis. **LIT in the LIGHTING WAVE, before the freeze — HORIZON's three doors, handed to the LIGHTING-INVENTORY lane BY NAME (the wave is chartered there; this volume names the slot, the doors, their lighting acts and their dormancy proofs):** (i) `demographicsEnabled` — lighting act: C4′ (every shipped preset INCLUDING the default `realistic_regional`, the `lit` record on the signature surface, the declaration by lane, the `soakRulesBaseline` rows); dormancy proof it leaves behind: the kernel trace, the four floor hashes and the lifecycle golden re-armed as the LIT world's, arm 42 green; pre-lighting evidence: the interim 4 s cell + the century leg's LIT arm + the kernel-only 300 y pin; cost: §2.5 C4′. (ii) `charsetPolicy.enforcement` — lighting act: `report → refuse` + regen + the ledger row (CS-2's veto row), ONLY after CS-9's restore path is green in the tree (⟦A6 P1 · A32 O1⟧ — CS-9 is on the wave's critical path); dormancy proof: every admission suite byte-identical under `report` and the own-export account-import plant green under `refuse`; cost: 0 eager bytes, and the paid surface stops printing `q` for `影`. (iii) `generationWorker` — lighting act: `FLAG_DEFAULTS.generationWorker: true` (WK-1), ONLY after the Playwright spec ⟦A11 P6⟧ is green; dormancy proof: the identity triple + golden 0/525 (ZERO golden movement — the lightest declared shift of the wave); cost: ≈ 20 B eager already priced, the first-generate wire bytes (WK-6). Each lights as a DECLARED SHIFT (ledger row + commit body + the bit-measured movement quoted; ⟦A8 P3⟧ no `docs/shift-records/*` file before the freeze); the lighting census is refrozen ONCE for the whole wave; the fences re-armed; the freeze then records the LIT world and no per-flag door signature is owed. The wave's RESIDUE — a lighting whose cost is a budget raise or a build program (the MAT dial's +1,047 B engine at 781 B RED per §879, `irregularForce` without SEAT-7/8, `warMemoryEnabled` until it has a lighting home, `characterDriftEnabled` if no rules overlay reaches it) — is an OWNER ROW with the cost and a recommendation to FUND it, never silently withheld and never silently raised (R9(d)); the inventory lists them. **Signed in the tuning pass (VALUES only, still LAST):** the tuning register v1 (TR-1; record A only if a value moved a golden); `DEMOGRAPHIC_TUNING_SIGNATURE.signed` (C5; arm 43 binds it to the 12 s receipt; CAP-10 rules a retune's re-run); the soak register's `research-lit` re-record (record C or its own ritual); `BAND_FLOOR_ROWS` signed (COV-1) if not at the freeze. Record B is RETIRED. **Seen in the walk, not lit there:** the unfrozen tab (WK-1's rehearsal), the lit default world; the walk's lighting classes are pre-answered YES (§881.4) and it keeps its non-lighting decisions. **What the review consumes:** §10.1's row — a LIT product. **What the terminal soak proves:** the 300-year LIT curve of the world the customer NOW gets PLATEAUS (the `research-lit` cell's `population.<id>.shape` = `plateau`, `realm.bifurcated` 0, `realm.ratio` in band) beside the DARK twin (run under the soak's `--lighting demographicsEnabled=false` overlay, since the shipped preset is lit) that FAILS `population_bounded` through the existing bounded check (⟦A7 P2⟧ the capacity rows NOT-EXECUTABLE on it); the liveness floor holds every decade on the LIT preset at 30/100/300 years (or the SOAK-4 red stands honestly until the sitting); the worker path's `jsonSha256 === direct` on the soak's isolate arm; the four capacity rows fire on nothing lit; the small-N stasis row read from the same run.

### §10.4 Parallel lanes
⟦A33 O2⟧⟦CHAIR-R2⟧ **The owner capped the board at FOUR running agents of ANY kind** (2026-09-02 00:26 ET, §881.3: build lanes, read-only skeptics and Workflow agents all count; a written queue `LANE-QUEUE.md` is pulled one item per freed slot). The plan as first written — seven probes at once, five build lanes at HORIZON-DARK, seven reader seats per campaign in parallel — could not be dispatched. **The four-slot schedule:** docks are cut per car from the boarding base named above; lanes run in parallel INSIDE the cap and DRAIN before their gate; ONE landing at a time. **G0**: two waves — wave 1 = WORKER Car 0 (the probe build, ALONE in its slot) + CHARSET Car 0 + WRWALKER Car 0 + COVERAGE Car 0; wave 2 = READERREVIEW Car 0 + SOAKCHAIN Car 0's measurement (+ CAPACITY C0, a chair act, no slot). **HORIZON-INSTR**: WRWALKER, TUNEREG, SOAKCHAIN build in parallel (three slots; the fourth is the chair's or a skeptic's) — disjoint files; the shared registers (lighting tuple, ratchet totals, mutation manifest) re-derived at the composed tip. **HORIZON-DARK**: waves ordered by critical path — wave 1 = CHARSET + WORKER (they share the closure margin, so their probe builds must see each other early) + CAPACITY + READERREVIEW; wave 2 = TUNEREG Cars 4–5 (+ COV-7(a)'s exports if signed) as slots free — the ONLY shared src file is `vite.config.js` (CHARSET's one `manualChunks` line; WORKER deliberately needs none; T13's edit is already landed), the shared test edits `engineChunkLazy.test.js` (WORKER arm 2) vs `vendorPdfLazy.test.js` (CAPACITY's sentinel arm) disjoint, and `densityCreateBoundary.js` (WORKER, ⟦A2 B2⟧) touched by no other lane. **The GOLDEN gate**: GOLDEN's lane + COVERAGE (two slots). **The reading (Car 3)**: a ROLLING QUEUE of ≤ 4 seats — 11 campaigns × 7 seats = 77 seat-readings plus refuters, four-wide: honestly ≈ 20 rounds of reading rather than the 11 parallel panels the volume first priced, i.e. the reading's wall-clock is ≈ 5× the single-panel figure, and the chair schedules it as such (RR-4's 'widen the roster' now costs four-wide time). Seats: Fable architects/validates every receipt; Opus implements (§724). The interrupt law: dispatch the critical-path lane of each gate ALONE; re-survey by transcript mtime after ANY interrupt.

| the review's fix consists | the review phase | ⟦A49 O18⟧ one composed gate (~16–25 min + HOLD) per car class per round, not per row — with N backlog rows in three classes ≈ 3–6 gates per review round; the reading itself ≈ 20 four-wide rounds (⟦A33 O2⟧ §10.4) | PLAUSIBLE |

---

## §REFUTE-ME — the five strongest attacks on the integrated design, and what survives

**Attack 1 — "Thirty-eight cars, nineteen test files, ~250 titles, five walkers and a second corpus build, all to land BEFORE a review whose findings you cannot predict. The estate's gate is already 16–25 minutes and the ratchet census is 10/10; you are spending the last of the program's slack on instruments and calling it the product."**
What survives: the owner's order is explicit — build and land EVERYTHING first, then the exhaustive review (§879.6), and TIME IS NOT THE CONSTRAINT; every instrument here answers a question the review would otherwise ask by hand (which bands the corpus exercises, which written facts reach a reader, which values are draft, whether the world moves, whether the paid renderers can draw the name) — and each is priced (§11.4: ≈ +13 min CPU, PLAUSIBLE +4–8 min wall-clock, no new chain step). What does NOT survive: the sum was never measured as a sum. The two long poles (the century leg, the second corpus build) are each a STOP-gated Car 0 figure, and §11.4's total is PLAUSIBLE arithmetic; if the HORIZON-INSTR gate's measured wall-clock — or ⟦A23 E7⟧ CI's ONE `check-tests` job, where every pole is cumulative under a 30-minute timeout — exceeds the estate's tolerance, the recorded folds (the `globalSetup` corpus cache, now a NAMED fallback; COVERAGE's six-row fallback; the century leg's seed count) are the cures, and none of them is a tier or a conditional step.

**Attack 2 — "The volume's sequencing was re-cut around a ruling that arrived after every design was written. Four designs reasoned about T13's 205 B; you struck that reasoning and put every src car AFTER T13 in one train. The train's byte proof is a single probe build of a composed tip whose base you have not seen, against a margin (~795 B) you derived by subtraction from a brief."**
What survives: the re-cut is marked at every site (`⟦INTEGRATOR-AMENDED⟧`, and the panel's `⟦A…⟧` folds), the lanes' vetoable JUDGMENTs are kept verbatim, and the ruling's arithmetic is now §881.3's MEASUREMENT (⟦A47 O16⟧ 504 B / 661 B at `594c7521f`) with the landing's build as the re-measurement (§14 E38, E40); every STOP rule the designs wrote against 396 B still holds, and the measured margin can only make them fire LESS often; ⟦A35 O4⟧ and the train's admission after T13 is not assumed but RULED under a BIT claim (§10.1 G5). The one-train shape is the estate's own law (a src-adding train pays the SEVEN-member scanner family and the four-census leaf bill ONCE; two trains pay twice) and E3's composition rehearsal at the composed tip is the measurement the coupling protocol demands. What does NOT survive: the claim that HORIZON-DARK could land after T13 by the integrator's own authority — it needed a chair ruling against the fence's seal law, which R1 now supplies (vetoable); the margin figure is no longer a derivation but a measurement, and the HORIZON-DARK probe build re-measures it at the boarding base before any car is cut.

**Attack 3 — "Three registers now claim the same dial (`_densityLawVersion`: COVERAGE's `dial-gated` exemption, WRWALKER's `generation-dial` row, TUNEREG's `signature-record` home for `REGISTER_VII_SIGNATURE`), two registers claim the same identity spelling without sharing a module, and the sitting produces up to three shift records. You have not unified the estate; you have tripled its bookkeeping and called the cross-arms 'integration'."**
What survives: the three registers claim three DIFFERENT things about one dial — that its bands are unexercised (COVERAGE), that its written keys are unread (WRWALKER), that its values are unsigned (TUNEREG) — and each claim is convicted by its own executed bit (the census, the lit-vs-dark corpus, the text digest); the both-ways arm (C20) makes two of them one act, and arm 34 makes the third agree with the record it indexes. The identity spelling is shared by an EXECUTED pin (WRWALKER's) that the others validate through the exported parser, which is how the estate already shares OSR's grammar without importing its governed files. The three records exist because the door's ONE-cause law exists (§764.3) and a single record hiding three causes is exactly the "a fix may declare ONE shifted curve and quietly move six others" hazard. What does NOT survive: the claim that this is cheap — every corpus-moving landing now re-freezes three registers (OSR, WRWALKER, COVERAGE) and the sitting signs three times; §11.2 prices it and X-1 asks the owner to confirm the shape.

**Attack 4 — "The bit claims are proved on the same thread, the same box, the same corpus. WORKER's identity triple runs a Worker double in the test's own engine; SOAKCHAIN's Tier 1 soaks a cardboard town; COVERAGE counts a stress-free one-seed corpus; READERREVIEW reads JSON dumps and calls them documents; WRWALKER's LIT is 'reachable by import', not 'shown'. Every instrument measures a proxy and the volume's thesis says 'SHOWN', 'EXERCISED', 'BYTE-IDENTICAL' as if the proxies were the thing."**
What survives: each proxy is named AS a proxy in its own §REFUTE-ME and carried into this volume unsoftened — WORKER ships the Node isolate (a real second engine with no `window`) and ⟦A11 P6⟧ no longer merely NAMES the missing browser e2e — it lands ONE Playwright spec as the lighting act's precondition; SOAKCHAIN asserts on the light fixture only what is fixture-independent and leaves magnitudes to the weekly generated watchdog and Tier 2; COVERAGE's floor is a REACHABILITY claim ("3 of 555, widened to the floor, not discovered at it") and the register keeps `rows` so a reader can see it; READERREVIEW's rubric scores CONTENT (did it show, can a reader say why with an address, do two documents contradict) under the parity contract that makes the view model the printed text, and assigns the visual layer to the walk explicitly; WRWALKER forbids the word "visible" in its register and grades `R`/`N` so the strength of every LIT is legible. What does NOT survive: any sentence in §0 that reads the eight as proving the product — they prove the ESTATE around the product; the product's own proof is the owner's walk and the owner's soak, which the arc places after all of this on purpose.

**Attack 5 — "Sixty-six owner rows. The design says 'decide within scope; record vetoably', then hands the owner a desk of sixty-six one-word questions, half of which the chair could rule under the 08-10 grant. A desk that long is a desk nobody clears, and the sitting stalls on CAP-9 while TR-8 rots."**
What survives: the rows are partitioned by NATURE — the carve-outs the grant names (the tuning signature TR-1/CAP-1, persisted shapes COV-3/CS-6/WK-2, new capability RR-2/WK-4/CAP-5, paid surfaces CAP-6/CS-3, budgets/ceilings WK-7/WK-10, infrastructure and cost SOAK-1/2, legal none) are the owner's by law and would be rows under any design; the rest are TASTE rows each design's brief demanded ("taste/policy") and each carries the chair's recommendation so the owner's act is one word or a veto. ⟦A46 O15⟧ The rows the recommendation's own logic already answers, and which the chair dispatches under the grant with a veto row — ENUMERATED, not 'fourteen': CS-3 (Car 3, declared by machinery), WRW-5 (the genesis bank), SOAK-3 (the cadence default), TR-6 (the phantom repair), WK-3 (concurrency unchanged), WK-8 (`duration_ms`), COV-4 (carto reported), COV-8 (no `safetyBand`), CS-4, CS-7, CS-8 (the charset defaults), RR-6 and RR-7 (the reports' home; two owner seeds) — thirteen, each carrying a default column in the collection table; CS-2 is NOT among them (it is HELD until CS-9 — ⟦A6 P1⟧); COV-1 (the floor) is OWNER-signed, not chair-dispatchable (§6.3 governs) — the chair does so at collection, and the desk shrinks to the by-nature rows. What does NOT survive: the count as a virtue. §12 is complete because the brief demanded completeness; the chair's collection act should present the by-nature rows first and the taste rows as a table with a default column, and the owner's reporting preference (a remaining-arc table when an item COMPLETES) is the shape it should take.

## §16 §REFUTE-ME, AMENDED — what survived the panel

The five attacks of §REFUTE-ME were written by the integrator against its own design; the panel then attacked the design with the tree in hand. What each attack's "survives" claim looks like after 63 findings:

**Attack 1 (the instruments spend the program's slack).** SURVIVED, weakened where it should be: the owner's order stands and every instrument still answers a review question by hand otherwise; but the panel found the sum was mis-attributed, not merely unmeasured — ⟦A23 E7⟧ CI runs ONE `check-tests` job, so the century leg, the second corpus build and the band census are cumulative under one 30-minute timeout, and the dev-box figure was the exclusive tier's while lane docks run capped at two workers. The cures (the `globalSetup` corpus cache, now a NAMED fallback; the seed count; COVERAGE's six-row switch) survive as the only lawful ones; §14 E41 is the measurement the volume now owes before HORIZON-INSTR boards.

**Attack 2 (the re-cut around a ruling; a margin derived from a brief).** DID NOT SURVIVE AS WRITTEN, and is better for it: the margin is no longer a derivation — ⟦A47 O16⟧ §881.3 MEASURED 504 B closure / 661 B engine at `594c7521f`; and the train's admission after T13 was not the integrator's to assume — ⟦A35 O4⟧ it needed a chair ruling against the fence's seal law, which R1 supplies as a BIT-claim admission (golden 0/525 + every fence identical at both arms; any movement a STOP), recorded against the arc line and vetoable. The one-train shape survives; the two instrument gates before T13 do not (⟦A34 O3⟧); COVERAGE rides the GOLDEN gate (⟦A36 O5⟧).

**Attack 3 (tripled bookkeeping).** SURVIVED, with the coupling made tighter than the volume claimed: the three registers still claim three different things about one dial, but ⟦A26 E10⟧ WRWALKER and OSR were 'one truth' by COUNT only until the `shapesDigest` pin; ⟦A9 P4⟧ the tuning register was a ledger beside the door until arm 33b made the converse a bit; and the sitting's records are now TWO, not three — ⟦CHAIR-R9⟧ the owner's lighting word retired record B by moving the capacity lighting before the freeze. What does NOT survive: 'the sitting signs three times' — it signs values, and the lightings were declared earlier.

**Attack 4 (proxies called bits).** DID NOT SURVIVE FOR WORKER AND CAPACITY, and the fold closed both: ⟦A11 P6⟧ the 'worker' leg of the identity triple was a hand-written double and the browser Worker path was an argument — ONE Playwright spec in the existing harness is now the lighting act's precondition; ⟦A16 P11⟧ the crowding line's cross-path identity ran on a dark campaign — one lit title makes it a bit; ⟦A7 P2⟧ the DARK twin's 'conviction' by the capacity rows could never have executed (NOT-EXECUTABLE under the C8 rider) — the existing bounded check convicts it and four planted receipts are the rows' negative control. For READERREVIEW the attack survived and grew: ⟦A52 L2⟧ four documents were model dumps a reader could cite as `shown`; ⟦A53 L3⟧ the validator could pass a report that answered nothing; ⟦A54 L4⟧ nothing scored register; ⟦A60 L9⟧ 'PROSE' could move lived history. Each is now machinery — SURFACE vs RECORD, `question_missing`, REGISTER, `PROSE_PERSISTED` — and the thesis sentence "read against a fixed rubric with checkable citations before anyone calls them legible" is now true of the rubric as specified rather than as hoped.

**Attack 5 (sixty-six owner rows).** SURVIVED as a partition, corrected as a count: 69 rows after the fold, three opened by the panel (CS-9 — the BLOCKING one, on the lighting wave's critical path; CAP-10; WRW-6b), five rewritten so the recommendation is the BEST option and not the cheapest (CS-1 EMBED, COV-7's raise offered plainly, TR-8's cull before the freeze, SOAK-1 with the ≈ 35 h summed, CAP-7's sequencing consequence stated), two turned from questions into records (CAP-2 — the owner's lighting ruling with its cost surfaced; RR-9 a standing law), and the 'fourteen chair-dispatchable' claim replaced by an enumerated thirteen with CS-2 explicitly NOT among them (⟦A46 O15⟧).

**What the panel found that the volume had not attacked itself — the two blockers.** ⟦A6 P1 · A32 O1⟧ a PROMISE breach on a non-authoring path: the design routed a user's OWN exported library through the charset wall on account import with an atomic whole-pack skip — one legacy name would have lost the library; cured by the RESTORE-path ruling (R3), owner row CS-9, and the lifecycle plant the design's proof 5 omitted. ⟦A51 L1⟧ the review's 'world the customer gets' never included the world the customer is HANDED — the default preset `realistic_regional` — and CAP-2 would have left it on the legacy population lane forever; cured by the roster pin (R4) and, decisively, by the owner's word (R9): every preset lights, the default first.

**What remains PLAUSIBLE after the panel** (§14, the rows the fold added): E41 the CI sum; ~~E42 the headless tab render~~; ~~E43 the worker bundle's measured size~~; E44 the interim 4 s cell's plateau before the wave; ~~E45 `PIPELINE_REACHERS` tree-shaken~~; E46 the src-wide roster's completeness. Each names its experiment and its STOP; none is argued.

⟦G0-93⟧ **AND AFTER THE FIVE G0 PROBES the list is shorter and the survivors are named honestly.** SETTLED by the wave: E42 (all three tabs render — mechanism proven, a real surface still owed to Car 2), E43 (1,404,723 B, chunk count **1**), E45 (tree-shaken from the WHOLE build, stronger than priced), plus E15 · E17 · E18 · E19 · E20 · E21 · E22-solo · E24 · E25 · E27 · E28 · E30 · E32 · E33 · E34 · E35 · E36 · E5-WRW · E5-RR, and E16 REFUTED in three parts plus its method. **What is genuinely still PLAUSIBLE, with its owner:** E41 the CI sum (read from the last green CI run before boarding) · E44 the interim 4 s cell's plateau (the manual run after SOAKCHAIN Car 4) · E46 the src-wide roster's completeness (every src car's WHOLE lint/copy/docs) · **E37 — the one row a probe was chartered for and did NOT settle** (the regen census extension was not in Car 0's act list; S3.1 stays ARMED) · E38 (its named instrument measures a base two landings later) · E23's remaining clause (Car 3's real widening run) · **E22's PARALLEL leg** (a property of a 558-file run, not of a probe) · **the four DERIVED lighting figures** of READERREVIEW Car 1 (the walker fails fast on `files`; the refreeze refuses on a dirty tree) · **whether espionage would EVER write at a larger realm scale** (four horizons falsified at four settlements; settlement count not varied). ⭐ **The wave's own lesson, banked: five of the six things that changed the INSTR cars most were not on this list at all** — they were roster rows, method clauses and bill lines the volume asserted without ever entering them in §14.
